Base

Universal Class Order (Mental Model)

🟢 Correct · 🟡 Caution · 🔴 Wrong

🟡 Demos are for concept understanding, not exact implementation.
🟡 Values and layout may be simplified for clarity.
🔴 Always adapt code to real use cases.

Interactive State (Mental Model)

🟢 s- represents a state — meaning the style applies only when something happens (hover, focus, active).
🟢 Examples: hs- = hover state, fs- = focus state, as- = active state.
🟢 Child variants: chs-, cfs-, cas- apply the state from a parent to its children.
🟢 These are interactive styles — they activate only during user interaction.
🟡 Without s, utilities are applied always (default/static state).
🔴 Do not use state prefixes for normal styling — use base utilities for consistent layout and design.

Framework Capability Matrix

🟢 Provides a high-level overview of every system in the framework.
🟢 Use this table to quickly determine which capabilities a system supports.
🟢 Sticky header and sticky first column make large matrices easier to navigate.
🟢 In table _ = not applicable.
🟡 Detailed behavior, scales, and examples remain documented within each system page.
🔴 Capability support does not imply identical implementation patterns across systems.

Normalize Presets

* {
  scroll-behavior: smooth;
  box-sizing: border-box;
  margin: 0;
  padding: 0;
}

body {
  font-family: monospace, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif;
  background: #f5f5f5;
  color: #111;
  line-height: 1.2;
}

.inset-0 {
  inset: 0;
}

table {
  border-collapse: collapse;
  border-spacing: 0;
}

td,
th {
  padding: 0;
}

a {
  text-decoration: none;
  color: inherit;
}

img {
  display: block;
  max-width: 100%;
  height: auto;
}

🟢 scroll-behavior Enables smooth scrolling for anchor navigation
🟢 Sets a consistent box model using border-box.
🟢 Removes default margin and padding across elements.
🟢 Applies a clean system font stack with neutral background and text.
🟡 Global resets may affect third-party components — use with awareness.

Aspect Ratio System

Aspect Ratio Utility Classes

.ar-auto { aspect-ratio: auto;  }
.ar-1x1  { aspect-ratio: 1 / 1; }
.ar-2x3  { aspect-ratio: 2 / 3; }
.ar-3x2  { aspect-ratio: 3 / 2; }
.ar-4x3  { aspect-ratio: 4 / 3; }
.ar-9x16 { aspect-ratio: 9 / 16;}
.ar-16x9 { aspect-ratio: 16 / 9;}
.ar-21x9 { aspect-ratio: 21 / 9;}

🟢 aspect ratio (ar-*) defines intrinsic layout space before image loads.
🔴 aspect-ratio must be applied to containers, not replaced elements (img, video, iframe)
🟢 child media typically requires w-100p and h-100p to fully fill the ratio container
🟡 Without ar-*, images use natural sizing (auto).
🟡 object-position (obj-*) can be used to control focal cropping with obj-cover

  <img class="w-100p h-100p obj-cover" src="">

Vertical Media

🟢 ar-* defines layout space before media loads
🔴 must only be applied to containers (not img/video/iframe)
🟢 ensures predictable, stable layouts (prevents layout shift)
🟢 child media typically requires w-100p and h-100p to fully fill the container
🟡 mismatched media ratios will cause cropping when used with obj-cover

Border System

Variables

:root {
  --ba-xxs: 1px;
  --ba-xs : 3px;
  --ba-sm : 5px;
  --ba-md : 7px;
  --ba-lg : 10px;
  --ba-xl : 15px;
  --ba-xxl: 20px;
}

Border Engine

[class^="ba-"], [class*=" ba-"],
[class^="bt-"], [class*=" bt-"],
[class^="br-"], [class*=" br-"],
[class^="bb-"], [class*=" bb-"],
[class^="bl-"], [class*=" bl-"] {
  border-width: 0;
  border-style: solid;
  border-color: var(--border-color, currentColor);
}

Border Utility Classes

.ba-xxs { border-width: var(--ba-xxs); }
.ba-xs  { border-width: var(--ba-xs);  }
.ba-sm  { border-width: var(--ba-sm);  }
.ba-md  { border-width: var(--ba-md);  }
.ba-lg  { border-width: var(--ba-lg);  }
.ba-xl  { border-width: var(--ba-xl);  }
.ba-xxl { border-width: var(--ba-xxl); }
.ba-0   { border-width: 0;             }
.bt-xxs { border-top-width: var(--ba-xxs); }
.bt-xs  { border-top-width: var(--ba-xs);  }
.bt-sm  { border-top-width: var(--ba-sm);  }
.bt-md  { border-top-width: var(--ba-md);  }
.bt-lg  { border-top-width: var(--ba-lg);  }
.bt-xl  { border-top-width: var(--ba-xl);  }
.bt-xxl { border-top-width: var(--ba-xxl); }
.bt-0   { border-top-width: 0;             }
.br-xxs { border-right-width: var(--ba-xxs); }
.br-xs  { border-right-width: var(--ba-xs);  }
.br-sm  { border-right-width: var(--ba-sm);  }
.br-md  { border-right-width: var(--ba-md);  }
.br-lg  { border-right-width: var(--ba-lg);  }
.br-xl  { border-right-width: var(--ba-xl);  }
.br-xxl { border-right-width: var(--ba-xxl); }
.br-0   { border-right-width: 0;             }
.bb-xxs { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-xxs); }
.bb-xs  { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-xs);  }
.bb-sm  { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-sm);  }
.bb-md  { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-md);  }
.bb-lg  { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-lg);  }
.bb-xl  { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-xl);  }
.bb-xxl { border-bottom-width: var(--ba-xxl); }
.bb-0   { border-bottom-width: 0;             }
.bl-xxs { border-left-width: var(--ba-xxs); }
.bl-xs  { border-left-width: var(--ba-xs);  }
.bl-sm  { border-left-width: var(--ba-sm);  }
.bl-md  { border-left-width: var(--ba-md);  }
.bl-lg  { border-left-width: var(--ba-lg);  }
.bl-xl  { border-left-width: var(--ba-xl);  }
.bl-xxl { border-left-width: var(--ba-xxl); }
.bl-0   { border-left-width: 0;             }

🟢 ba-* utilities define border thickness using a semantic scale (xxs → xxl) for clarity and consistency.
🟢 Directional utilities (bt-, br-, bb-, bl-) control border sides individually.
🟢 Borders are inactive by default (border-width: 0) and apply only when explicitly used.
🟢 Border uses currentColor by default
🟢 bclr-* can override border color independently which include hs-bclr-* and chs-bclr-*
🔴 clr-* affects text color while bclr-* affects only border color
🟡 Each side is controlled independently — no fallback or shared base is used
🔴 Border-width is not used in hover interactions as it does not transition smoothly
🔴 Avoid using hs-* or chs-* with border-width — results may feel abrupt or inconsistent

Helper Classes

.bstyle-solid  { border-style: solid;  }
.bstyle-dashed { border-style: dashed; }
.bstyle-dotted { border-style: dotted; }
.bstyle-double { border-style: double; }
.bstyle-none   { border-style: none;   }

🟢 bstyle-* defines border style independently from width
🟢 Works alongside border to form complete border structure
🟡 Border style changes are immediate and do not transition smoothly
🔴 Avoid decorative styles like groove or ridge — inconsistent across browsers

Scale (xxs → xxl)

ba-0
ba-xxs
ba-xs

🟢 ba-* applies border on all sides
🟢 bt, br, bb, bl control individual sides
🟢 Uses a semantic scale (xxs → xxl)
🟡 Border color follows clr-*
🔴 Border width is structural and does not change on interaction

bstyle-* (Border Style)

bstyle-solid
bstyle-dashed
bstyle-dotted
bstyle-double

🟢 bstyle-* defines border style independently
🟢 Combines with ba-* to form the border
🟡 Style changes are static and not interactive
🔴 Border structure (width/style) remains fixed

Self Hover (.hs-clr-*)

Gray → Main

ba-md clr-g4 hs-clr-main
Gray → Sub

bl-lg bb-sm clr-g5 hs-clr-sub
Gray → Acnt

bt-md br-xxl clr-g6 hs-clr-acnt

🟢 Border uses currentColor internally
🟢 hs-clr-* updates border color on hover
🟡 Interaction is smooth since only color changes
🔴 Border width and style remain unchanged

Parent Hover (.chs-clr-*)

Parent Hover Area (hover this box)

chs-parent

chs-clr-main

Abc

clr-g4 → chs-clr-main

chs-clr-sub

Abc

clr-g5 → chs-clr-sub

chs-clr-acnt

Abc

clr-g6 → chs-clr-acnt

🟢 Hover this container to apply parent interaction
🟢 chs-clr-* applies color to children on parent hover
🟡 All cards update together as a group
🔴 Requires chs-parent to activate
🔴 Only color changes — structure remains unchanged

Color System

Variables

:root {
--color-main    : hsl(244, 100%, 68%);
--color-main-h  : hsl(244, 100%, 78%);
--color-sub     : hsl(124, 100%, 68%);
--color-sub-h   : hsl(124, 100%, 78%);
--color-acnt    : hsl(14, 100%, 68%);
--color-acnt-h  : hsl(14, 100%, 78%);
--color-black   : hsl(0, 0%, 0%);
--color-g9      : hsl(0, 0%, 10%);
--color-g8      : hsl(0, 0%, 20%);
--color-g7      : hsl(0, 0%, 30%);
--color-g6      : hsl(0, 0%, 40%);
--color-g5      : hsl(0, 0%, 50%);
--color-g4      : hsl(0, 0%, 60%);
--color-g3      : hsl(0, 0%, 70%);
--color-g2      : hsl(0, 0%, 80%);
--color-g1      : hsl(0, 0%, 90%);
--color-white   : hsl(0, 0%, 100%);
--color-0       : hsla(0, 0%, 100%, 0.00);
}

.bg-0      { background-color: var(--color-0);      }

🟢 Base (clr-*, bg-*) set default color
🟢 Parent (chs-*) applies on parent hover
🟢 Self (hs-*) applies on hover and overrides parent
🟢 Color transitions work smoothly with ts-* utilities
🟡 Last class wins when conflicts occur
🔴 Colors do not stack — only one applies

Hover Engine

[class*=" hs-clr-"]:hover,
[class^="hs-clr-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class^="hs-clr-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" hs-clr-"]:hover {
  color: var(--hs-clr);
}
[class*=" hs-bg-"]:hover,
[class^="hs-bg-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class^="hs-bg-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" hs-bg-"]:hover {
  background-color: var(--hs-bg);
}

🟢 hs-* is written with extra specificity to ensure it works correctly inside parent hover.
     .hs-*:hover,
     .chs-parent:hover .hs-*:hover {...}
🟢 It allows self hover to work even when chs-parent + chs-* is active
🟡 This keeps behavior consistent across shadow, opacity, color and Other systems
🔴 Without this, chs-* will override hs-* and self hover will not work

Child Hover Engine

.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-clr-"],
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" chs-clr-"] {
  color: var(--chs-clr);
}
.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-bg-"],
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" chs-bg-"] {
  background-color: var(--chs-bg);
}

🟢 Defines the core color palette using CSS variables.
🟡 Changes at root level affect the entire system — use with awareness.
🟢 clr-* utilities apply text color consistently.
🟢 bg-* utilities apply background color using the same system.
🔴 Avoid using inline colors — breaks system consistency.
🟢 State variants (-h) are used for interaction states (hover, active).
🟢 --color-0 provides a transparent base value.
🟡 Transparency may expose underlying layers — use carefully.

🟢 hs-* applies hover styles using your color system.
🟢 Combine with ts-* for smooth transitions.
🟡 No duplicate “hover cards” needed — interaction is real.
🔴 Color values are separate from how hover works

Parent & Self Hover (.chs-parent .chs-bg-* .hs-bg-*)

Parent Hover Area (hover this box)

chs-parent

Main (parent hover)

Abc

bg-g2  → chs-bg-main
clr-g8 → chs-clr-white

Sub (parent hover)

Abc

bg-g2  → chs-bg-sub
clr-g8 → chs-clr-white

Acnt (parent hover)

Abc

bg-g2  → chs-bg-acnt
clr-g8 → chs-clr-white

🟢 Hover anywhere in this section to apply colors to all cards
🟢 chs-* changes child elements when parent is hovered
🔴 Child elements must use chs-* to respond to parent hover
🟡 All cards update together on parent hover
🔴 Without chs-parent, this will not work

Parent + Self Interaction

chs-parent

Parent: main
Self: acnt

Abc

       → hs-bg-acnt
clr-g8 → hs-clr-white

Self: main

       → hs-bg-main
clr-g8 → hs-clr-white

Parent: acnt
Self: g1

Abc

bg-g2  → chs-bg-acnt
       → hs-bg-white
clr-g8 → hs-clr-acnt

🟢 chs-* applies color when you hover the parent (group interaction)
🟢 hs-* applies color when you hover the card itself (self interaction)
🟡 Parent hover sets a base color, self hover updates it for that card
🔴 hs-* is written stronger to override chs-* when both are active
🔴 Color values replace each other, they do not layer or blend

Cursor System

Cursor Utility Classes

.cur-auto     { cursor: auto;        }
.cur-default  { cursor: default;     }
.cur-pointer  { cursor: pointer;     }
.cur-text     { cursor: text;        }
.cur-grab     { cursor: grab;        }
.cur-grabbing { cursor: grabbing;    }
.cur-na       { cursor: not-allowed; }
.cur-wait     { cursor: wait;        }
.cur-progress { cursor: progress;    }
.cur-zoom-in  { cursor: zoom-in;     }
.cur-zoom-out { cursor: zoom-out;    }
.cur-none     { cursor: none;        }

Helper Classes

button, a, [role="button"] { cursor: pointer;     }
:disabled, [disabled]      { cursor: not-allowed; }

🟢 cur-* utilities control cursor behavior (interaction feedback), not layout or spacing.
🟢 cur-pointer is used for clickable elements (buttons, links, cards).
🟢 cur-text is used for editable content (inputs, text areas).
🟢 cur-na indicates disabled or blocked actions.
🟡 Should match real interaction — cursor must reflect actual behavior.
🟡 Many elements (button, input, link) already have default cursor behavior.
🔴 Avoid adding pointer to non-clickable elements — it creates misleading UX.

cur-pointer

cur-grabbing
cur-na
cur-wait

Interactive States

Drag (hold click) - grab / grabbing

🟢 Cursor reflects interaction type (click, edit, drag)
🟡 Use pointer only on clickable elements
🟢 Buttons and links already use pointer by default
🟢 Add cursor only when behavior is not obvious
🟢 cur-grab indicates draggable elements
🟢 cur-grabbing appears while actively dragging (mousedown)
🟢 disabled automatically applies not-allowed + reduced opacity Added for clarity
🔴 Do not fake drag or disabled states — must reflect real behavior

Display System

Display Utility Classes

.dis-block        { display: block;        }
.dis-inline       { display: inline;       }
.dis-inline-block { display: inline-block; }
.dis-flex         { display: flex;         }
.dis-inline-flex  { display: inline-flex;  }
.dis-grid         { display: grid;         }
.dis-inline-grid  { display: inline-grid;  }
.dis-none         { display: none;         }
.dis-contents     { display: contents;     }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.dis-tb-block        { display: block;        }
.dis-tb-inline       { display: inline;       }
.dis-tb-inline-block { display: inline-block; }
.dis-tb-flex         { display: flex;         }
.dis-tb-inline-flex  { display: inline-flex;  }
.dis-tb-grid         { display: grid;         }
.dis-tb-inline-grid  { display: inline-grid;  }
.dis-tb-none         { display: none;         }
.dis-tb-contents     { display: contents;     }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.dis-mb-block        { display: block;        }
.dis-mb-inline       { display: inline;       }
.dis-mb-inline-block { display: inline-block; }
.dis-mb-flex         { display: flex;         }
.dis-mb-inline-flex  { display: inline-flex;  }
.dis-mb-grid         { display: grid;         }
.dis-mb-inline-grid  { display: inline-grid;  }
.dis-mb-none         { display: none;         }
.dis-mb-contents     { display: contents;     }
}

Hover Engine

[class*=" hs-dis-"]:hover,
[class^="hs-dis-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class^="hs-dis-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" hs-dis-"]:hover {
  display: var(--hs-dis);
}

Hover Utility Classes

.hs-dis-block        { --hs-dis: block;        }
.hs-dis-inline       { --hs-dis: inline;       }
.hs-dis-inline-block { --hs-dis: inline-block; }
.hs-dis-flex         { --hs-dis: flex;         }
.hs-dis-inline-flex  { --hs-dis: inline-flex;  }
.hs-dis-grid         { --hs-dis: grid;         }
.hs-dis-inline-grid  { --hs-dis: inline-grid;  }
.hs-dis-none         { --hs-dis: none;         }
.hs-dis-contents     { --hs-dis: contents;     }

Child Hover Engine

.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-dis-"],
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" chs-dis-"] {
  display: var(--chs-dis);
}

Child Hover Utility Classes

.chs-dis-block        { --chs-dis: block;        }
.chs-dis-inline       { --chs-dis: inline;       }
.chs-dis-inline-block { --chs-dis: inline-block; }
.chs-dis-flex         { --chs-dis: flex;         }
.chs-dis-inline-flex  { --chs-dis: inline-flex;  }
.chs-dis-grid         { --chs-dis: grid;         }
.chs-dis-inline-grid  { --chs-dis: inline-grid;  }
.chs-dis-none         { --chs-dis: none;         }
.chs-dis-contents     { --chs-dis: contents;     }

🟢 dis-* utilities define how elements participate in layout (block, inline, flex, grid).
🟢 Controls whether elements stack, flow inline, or create layout systems.
🟢 Used to activate layout engines like flex and grid.
🟡 Responsive variants (dis-tb-*, dis-mb-*) allow layout changes across devices.
🟡 dis-none removes elements completely (no space, no rendering).
🔴 Display cannot be animated using transition — use opacity/visibility for transitions
🟡 dis-contents removes the wrapper but keeps its children.
🟡 Use display changes for structural toggling (menus, dropdowns)
🟡 Avoid using display for visual transitions (use opacity/visibility instead)
🔴 Elements with display: none are not accessible (no keyboard or screen reader access)
🔴 Flex and Grid have their own layout systems — avoid mixing display with layout utilities unnecessarily.
🟢 Base (dis-*) sets default layout behavior
🟢 Parent (chs-dis-*) applies on parent hover
🟢 Self (hs-dis-*) applies on hover and overrides parent
🔴 hs-dis-* overrides chs-dis-* when both are active
🟡 Last class wins when multiple display utilities are used
🔴 Display values do not stack — only one display applies

🟢 dis-flex is one-directional (row/column).

🟢 dis-none removes the element completely.

🟢 dis-block → wrapper exists and controls layout.
🟢 dis-contents → wrapper disappears, only children remain.
🟡 Children behave as if they moved to the parent level.
🔴 Wrapper styles (background, padding, border) are lost.

Flex System

Presets

.flex-x, .flex-y { 
    display: flex;    
}

Helper Classes

.f-fill    { flex: 1;           }
.f-wrap    { flex-wrap: wrap;   }
.f-no-wrap { flex-wrap: nowrap; }

🟢 Flex system controls flow and alignment, not structure or spacing.
🟢 Combine with grid for layout structure and gap / spacing for spacing.
🔴 Using flex for everything may reduce clarity and maintainability.
🟢 flex-x / flex-y create flex containers with row or column flow.
🟡 f-fill allows items to grow and occupy available space.
🟢 Items align to start by default and wrap when needed.
🟡 Use f-no-wrap to force a single line layout.

Flex Direction Utility Classes

.flex-x { flex-direction: row;    }
.flex-y { flex-direction: column; }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.flex-x-tb { flex-direction: row;    }
.flex-y-tb { flex-direction: column; }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.flex-x-mb { flex-direction: row;    }
.flex-y-mb { flex-direction: column; }
}

🟢 flex-x = horizontal flow · flex-y = vertical flow.
🟢 Direction defines the main axis of the layout.
🟡 Responsive variants (tb / mb) override direction at breakpoints.
🔴 Changing direction changes how alignment behaves — use with awareness.

Flex Grow / Shrink Utility Classes

.f-grow        { flex-grow: 1; }
.f-grow-none   { flex-grow: 0; }

.f-shrink      { flex-shrink: 1; }
.f-shrink-none { flex-shrink: 0; }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.f-tb-grow        { flex-grow: 1; }
.f-tb-grow-none   { flex-grow: 0; }

.f-tb-shrink      { flex-shrink: 1; }
.f-tb-shrink-none { flex-shrink: 0; }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.f-mb-grow        { flex-grow: 1; }
.f-mb-grow-none   { flex-grow: 0; }

.f-mb-shrink      { flex-shrink: 1; }
.f-mb-shrink-none { flex-shrink: 0; }
}

🟢 f-fill is often the simpler alternative when the item should occupy all remaining space.
🟢 f-grow allows a flex item to consume available free space.
🟢 f-grow-none prevents automatic expansion.
🟢 f-shrink allows a flex item to shrink when necessary.
🟢 f-shrink-none prevents shrinking and preserves intrinsic size.
🟡 Useful for sidebars, logos, navigation items and toolbars.
🔴 Grow and shrink affect only flex items, not regular elements.

Flex (x-axis/align-items) Utility Classes

.f-left     { align-items: flex-start; }
.f-center   { align-items: center;     }
.f-right    { align-items: flex-end;   }
.f-stretch  { align-items: stretch;    }
.f-baseline { align-items: baseline;   }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.f-tb-left     { align-items: flex-start; }
.f-tb-center   { align-items: center;     }
.f-tb-right    { align-items: flex-end;   }
.f-tb-stretch  { align-items: stretch;    }
.f-tb-baseline { align-items: baseline;   }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.f-mb-left     { align-items: flex-start; }
.f-mb-center   { align-items: center;     }
.f-mb-right    { align-items: flex-end;   }
.f-mb-stretch  { align-items: stretch;    }
.f-mb-baseline { align-items: baseline;   }
}

🟢 f-left / f-center / f-right align items on the cross axis.
🟡 Alignment depends on flex direction:
     - In flex-x → f-left/f-center/f-right align vertically
     - In flex-y → f-left/f-center/f-right align horizontally
🟢 In flex-x → vertical alignment · in flex-y → horizontal alignment.
🟢 f-stretch expands items to fill available cross-axis space.
🟢 f-baseline aligns items based on text baseline (useful for mixed font sizes).
🔴 Baseline alignment may appear inconsistent if content sizes vary heavily.
🔴 Misunderstanding axis direction may cause unexpected alignment results.

Flex (y-axis/justify-content) Utility Classes

.f-top    { justify-content: flex-start;    }
.f-middle { justify-content: center;        }
.f-bottom { justify-content: flex-end;      }
.f-spread { justify-content: space-between; }
.f-around { justify-content: space-around;  }
.f-even   { justify-content: space-evenly;  }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.f-tb-top    { justify-content: flex-start;    }
.f-tb-middle { justify-content: center;        }
.f-tb-bottom { justify-content: flex-end;      }
.f-tb-spread { justify-content: space-between; }
.f-tb-around { justify-content: space-around;  }
.f-tb-even   { justify-content: space-evenly;  }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.f-mb-top    { justify-content: flex-start;    }
.f-mb-middle { justify-content: center;        }
.f-mb-bottom { justify-content: flex-end;      }
.f-mb-spread { justify-content: space-between; }
.f-mb-around { justify-content: space-around;  }
.f-mb-even   { justify-content: space-evenly;  }
}

🟢 f-top / f-middle / f-bottom position items on the main axis.
🟡 f-top / f-middle / f-bottom always follow the main axis (direction)
🟢 f-spread → space between items only (no space on edges).
🟢 f-around → equal space around items (edges get half space).
🟢 f-even → equal spacing everywhere (including edges).
🔴 Do not mix positioning and spacing utilities at the same breakpoint.
🟡 Space distribution depends on available free space.

Flex (align-content) Utility Classes

.f-lines-top    { align-content: flex-start;    }
.f-lines-middle { align-content: center;        }
.f-lines-bottom { align-content: flex-end;      }
.f-lines-spread { align-content: space-between; }
.f-lines-around { align-content: space-around;  }
.f-lines-even   { align-content: space-evenly;  }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.f-tb-lines-top    { align-content: flex-start;    }
.f-tb-lines-middle { align-content: center;        }
.f-tb-lines-bottom { align-content: flex-end;      }
.f-tb-lines-spread { align-content: space-between; }
.f-tb-lines-around { align-content: space-around;  }
.f-tb-lines-even   { align-content: space-evenly;  }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.f-mb-lines-top    { align-content: flex-start;    }
.f-mb-lines-middle { align-content: center;        }
.f-mb-lines-bottom { align-content: flex-end;      }
.f-mb-lines-spread { align-content: space-between; }
.f-mb-lines-around { align-content: space-around;  }
.f-mb-lines-even   { align-content: space-evenly;  }
}

🟢 f-lines-* controls alignment of multiple flex rows/columns.
🟢 Works only when items wrap into more than one line.
🔴 Has no effect on single-line layouts.
🟡 Avoid mixing with align-items — may create conflicting alignment.

Flex (align-self) Utility Classes

.f-self-left     { align-self: flex-start; }
.f-self-center   { align-self: center;     }
.f-self-right    { align-self: flex-end;   }
.f-self-stretch  { align-self: stretch;    }
.f-self-baseline { align-self: baseline;   }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.f-tb-self-left     { align-self: flex-start; }
.f-tb-self-center   { align-self: center;     }
.f-tb-self-right    { align-self: flex-end;   }
.f-tb-self-stretch  { align-self: stretch;    }
.f-tb-self-baseline { align-self: baseline;   }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.f-mb-self-left     { align-self: flex-start; }
.f-mb-self-center   { align-self: center;     }
.f-mb-self-right    { align-self: flex-end;   }
.f-mb-self-stretch  { align-self: stretch;    }
.f-mb-self-baseline { align-self: baseline;   }
}

🟢 f-self-* overrides cross-axis alignment for a single flex item.
🟢 Takes precedence over the parent f-left/f-center/f-right alignment.
🟢 Useful for badges, buttons, avatars and featured items.
🟡 Alignment follows the cross axis of the current flex direction.
🔴 Only affects the individual item it is applied to.

Grid System

Grid Utility Classes

.grid {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(12, minmax(0, 1fr));
}

🟢 .grid creates a 12-column layout using equal fractions.
🟢 Grid items automatically flow left to right, top to bottom.
🟡 When total span exceeds 12, items wrap to the next row.
🟡 Grid system focuses on layout structure, not alignment or spacing.
🟡 Combine with flex or alignment utilities for content positioning.
🟡 Grid does not include spacing — use gap-*, padding, or margin explicitly.
🔴 Do not rely on grid for spacing — it only defines structure.

Grid Column Utility Classes

.g-1  { grid-column: span 1;  }
.g-2  { grid-column: span 2;  }
.g-3  { grid-column: span 3;  }
.g-4  { grid-column: span 4;  }
.g-5  { grid-column: span 5;  }
.g-6  { grid-column: span 6;  }
.g-7  { grid-column: span 7;  }
.g-8  { grid-column: span 8;  }
.g-9  { grid-column: span 9;  }
.g-10 { grid-column: span 10; }
.g-11 { grid-column: span 11; }
.g-12 { grid-column: span 12; }
@media (max-width: 990px) {

.g-tb-2  { grid-column: span 2;  }
.g-tb-3  { grid-column: span 3;  }
.g-tb-4  { grid-column: span 4;  }
.g-tb-5  { grid-column: span 5;  }
.g-tb-6  { grid-column: span 6;  }
.g-tb-7  { grid-column: span 7;  }

.g-tb-10 { grid-column: span 10; }

.g-mb-11 { grid-column: span 11; }
.g-mb-12 { grid-column: span 12; }
}

🟢 g-* utilities control column span across the 12-column system.
🟢 Responsive variants (g-tb-*, g-mb-*) override spans at breakpoints.
🔴 Avoid assigning spans beyond 12 — breaks layout consistency.

Grid Row Utility Classes

.gy-1  { grid-row: span 1;  }

🟢 gy-* utilities control row span for vertical sizing.
🟢 Rows expand naturally based on content height.
🔴 Row spans do not fix height — content still defines final height.

Filter System

Filter Engine

[class^="blur-"],           [class*=" blur-"],
[class^="brightness-"],     [class*=" brightness-"],
[class^="contrast-"],       [class*=" contrast-"],
[class^="grayscale"],       [class*=" grayscale"],
[class^="invert"],          [class*=" invert"],
[class^="saturate-"],       [class*=" saturate-"],
[class^="sepia"],           [class*=" sepia"],
[class^="hue-"],            [class*=" hue-"],
[class^="dsw-"],            [class*=" dsw-"],

[class^="ho:blur-"],        [class*=" ho:blur-"],
[class^="ho:brightness-"],  [class*=" ho:brightness-"],
[class^="ho:contrast-"],    [class*=" ho:contrast-"],
[class^="ho:grayscale"],    [class*=" ho:grayscale"],
[class^="ho:invert"],       [class*=" ho:invert"],
[class^="ho:saturate-"],    [class*=" ho:saturate-"],
[class^="ho:sepia"],        [class*=" ho:sepia"],
[class^="ho:hue-"],         [class*=" ho:hue-"],
[class^="ho:dsw-"],         [class*=" ho:dsw-"],

[class^="cho:blur-"],       [class*=" cho:blur-"],
[class^="cho:brightness-"], [class*=" cho:brightness-"],
[class^="cho:contrast-"],   [class*=" cho:contrast-"],
[class^="cho:grayscale"],   [class*=" cho:grayscale"],
[class^="cho:invert"],      [class*=" cho:invert"],
[class^="cho:saturate-"],   [class*=" cho:saturate-"],
[class^="cho:sepia"],       [class*=" cho:sepia"],
[class^="cho:hue-"],        [class*=" cho:hue-"],
[class^="cho:dsw-"],        [class*=" cho:dsw-"]{
  filter:
    blur(var(--blur, 0px))
    brightness(var(--brightness, 100%))
    contrast(var(--contrast, 100%))
    drop-shadow(var(--dsw, 0 0 0 rgba(0,0,0,0)))
    grayscale(var(--grayscale, 0%))
    hue-rotate(var(--hue, 0deg))
    invert(var(--invert, 0%))
    saturate(var(--saturate, 100%))
    sepia(var(--sepia, 0%));
}

🟢 brightness-*p adjusts visual brightness using percentages
🟢 Supports values from 0%–200%
🟢 brightness-100p represents the original brightness
🟢 Values below 100 darken the element
🟢 Values above 100 brighten the element
🟢 Can be combined with all other Filter utilities
🟡 Brightness affects rendered appearance only — does not affect layout
🔴 Multiple Brightness utilities do not combine — the last Brightness utility wins

Drop-shadow Variables

:root {
  --dsw-xxs: 2px 2px 2px  rgba(0, 0, 0);
  --dsw-xs : 3px 3px 3px  rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.9);
  --dsw-sm : 4px 4px 4px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.8);
  --dsw-md : 6px 6px 6px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.7);
  --dsw-lg : 8px 8px 8px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.6);
  --dsw-xl : 12px 12px 12px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.5);
  --dsw-xxl: 18px 18px 18px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.5);
  --dsw-0  : 0 0 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.0);
}

Drop-shadow Utility Classes

.dsw-xxs { --dsw: var(--dsw-xxs); }
.dsw-xs  { --dsw: var(--dsw-xs);  }
.dsw-sm  { --dsw: var(--dsw-sm);  }
.dsw-md  { --dsw: var(--dsw-md);  }
.dsw-lg  { --dsw: var(--dsw-lg);  }
.dsw-xl  { --dsw: var(--dsw-xl);  }
.dsw-xxl { --dsw: var(--dsw-xxl); }
.dsw-0   { --dsw: var(--dsw-0);   }

  --dsw: var(--hs-dsw);
}

.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-dsw-"],

  --dsw: var(--chs-dsw);
}

Drop-shadow Child Hover Utility Classes

.chs-dsw-xxs { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-xxs); }
.chs-dsw-xs  { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-xs);  }
.chs-dsw-sm  { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-sm);  }
.chs-dsw-md  { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-md);  }
.chs-dsw-lg  { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-lg);  }
.chs-dsw-xl  { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-xl);  }
.chs-dsw-xxl { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-xxl); }
.chs-dsw-0   { --chs-dsw: var(--dsw-0);   }

🟢 dsw-* utilities apply filter-based drop shadows
🟢 Uses the same elevation scale as the Shadow System (xxs → xxl)
🟢 Drop Shadow follows image transparency and element shape
🟢 Can be combined with Blur, Brightness, Contrast, Hue, Saturate and other Filters
🟢 hs-dsw-* applies Drop Shadow on self hover
🟢 chs-dsw-* applies Drop Shadow from a chs-parent hover
🟡 Drop Shadow is rendered through the Filter System, not box-shadow
🟡 Best suited for images, icons, SVGs and transparent elements
🔴 Multiple dsw-* utilities do not combine — the last Drop Shadow utility wins

Drop-shadow Live Demo

Drop Shadow Levels (.dsw-*)

dsw-0

dsw-xxs

dsw-xs

dsw-sm

dsw-md

dsw-lg

dsw-xl

dsw-xxl

🟢 dsw-* defines the base drop shadow level
🟢 Drop shadow follows the visible PNG/SVG shape instead of the image rectangle
🟡 Best for logos, icons, stickers, product cutouts and transparent assets
🔴 Drop shadow affects appearance only — it does not affect layout or spacing

Self Hover (.hs-dsw-*)

Normal
hs-dsw-lg

dsw-xxs
hs-dsw-0

dsw-xs
hs-dsw-xl

dsw-xxl
hs-dsw-sm

🟢 hs-dsw-* applies a drop shadow on element hover
🟢 Useful for product images, icons, PNG illustrations and avatars
🟡 Creates elevation without affecting image dimensions
🔴 Only one drop shadow value applies at a time

Parent & Self Hover (.chs-parent .chs-dsw-* .hs-dsw-*)

Parent Hover Area

chs-parent

🟢 chs-parent creates a shared hover trigger
🟢 chs-dsw-* changes image shadows when the parent is hovered
🟢 Ideal for galleries, product grids and grouped media cards
🔴 Every matching child responds to the same parent hover event

Parent sets, Self refines

chs-parent

🟢 chs-dsw-* defines group-level drop shadow behavior
🟢 hs-dsw-* defines element-level refinement
🟢 Self hover always overrides parent hover when active
🟡 All interactions modify the same drop-shadow variable (--dsw)
🔴 Drop shadow is a single-value property — values do not stack

🟢 grayscale applies full grayscale (100%)
🟢 grayscale-none removes grayscale (0%)
🟢 Ideal for logos, client showcases, team cards and gallery effects
🟢 Can be combined with all other Filter utilities

🔴 Grayscale utilities do not combine — the last Grayscale utility wins

🟢 hue-* rotates colors around the hue wheel
🟢 Supports 0°–360° in 10° increments
🟢 Supports additive precision through hue-add-*
🟢 Example: hue-40deg + hue-add-5deg = 45deg
🟢 Can be combined with Blur, Brightness, Contrast, Saturate and other Filters
🟢 hs-hue-* applies Hue Rotate on self hover
🟢 Supports additive precision through hs-hue-add-*
🟢 chs-hue-* applies Hue Rotate from a chs-parent hover
🟢 Supports additive precision through chs-hue-add-*
🔴 Multiple Hue utilities do not combine — the last Hue utility wins

Height System

Variables

:root {  
  --h-a-px: 0px;
  --h-a-p: 0%;
  --h-a-vh: 0vh;
  --h-a-min: 0px;
  --h-a-max: 0px;
}

Height Engine

[class^="h-"],[class*=" h-"] {
  height: var(--h-height, auto);
  max-height: var(--h-max, none);
  min-height: var(--h-min, auto);
}

Helper Classes

.h-auto     { 
    height: auto;        
    max-height: none;
    min-height: 0;
}

.h-fit      { 
    height: fit-content; 
    max-height: none;
    min-height: 0;
}

🟢 Any h-* class activates the height system
🟢 Each system (height / max / min) operates independently
🟢 Height is composed using a base (h-*) + additive values (h-add-*)
🟢 Use only one base class per element
🟢 Add classes refine the nearest base value using 1-unit increments
🟡 Use only when necessary — height affects layout strongly
🟡 Height defines available space — content may overflow, be clipped, or scroll
🟡 min/max constraints override height when limits are reached
🟡 Avoid combining conflicting constraints (e.g., very small max with large min)
🔴 Avoid stacking multiple base h-* classes

px | min | max - Utility Classes

List System

List Utility Classes

.li-disc    { list-style-type: disc;        }
.li-circle  { list-style-type: circle;      }
.li-square  { list-style-type: square;      }

.li-decimal { list-style-type: decimal;     }
.li-lalpha  { list-style-type: lower-alpha; }
.li-ualpha  { list-style-type: upper-alpha; }
.li-lroman  { list-style-type: lower-roman; }
.li-uroman  { list-style-type: upper-roman; }

.li-none    { list-style-type: none;        }
.li-inside  { list-style-position: inside;  }
.li-outside { list-style-position: outside; }

🟢 li-none removes list markers and is commonly used for menus, navigation and custom components
🟢 li-disc, li-circle and li-square provide unordered list markers
🟢 li-decimal, li-lalpha, li-ualpha, li-lroman and li-uroman provide ordered list markers
🟢 li-inside places markers inside the content flow
🟢 li-outside places markers outside the content flow and is the browser default
🟡 List marker styles rarely need responsive variants and are typically consistent across all screen sizes
🟡 List marker utilities can be applied to both <ul> and <ol> elements
🟡 Marker appearance is controlled by CSS while list semantics are determined by the HTML element
🟢 Use unordered markers (disc, circle, square) with <ul> and ordered markers (decimal, alpha, roman) with <ol> for best semantic accessibility
🔴 List utilities affect marker appearance and marker positioning only. Item spacing, indentation and layout are controlled by spacing and layout utilities.

Custom List Content Variables

:root {
  --li-symbol: "→";
  --li-img: url("https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/bootstrap-icons@1.11.3/icons/star.svg");
}

List Content Utility Classes

.li-symbol, .li-img {
  list-style:none;
}

.li-symbol li::before {
  content:var(--li-symbol);
  margin-right:10px;
}


.li-img li::before {
  content:var(--li-img);
  margin-right:10px;
}

🟢 li-symbol replaces default list markers with a custom symbol defined through the --li-symbol variable
🟢 li-img replaces default list markers with a custom image defined through the --li-img variable
🟢 Symbols and images can be changed per project without modifying framework utilities
🟢 Useful for checklists, navigation menus, feature lists, branding elements and custom UI components
🟢 Supports project-specific SVG, PNG, WebP and other browser-supported image formats through CSS variables
🟡 The framework provides the rendering engine while the project controls the displayed symbol or image through variables
🟢 Additional marker styles can be introduced by updating variables rather than creating new utility classes
🔴 List content utilities affect marker appearance only and do not control spacing, indentation or layout behavior

  • Accessible Components
  • Reusable Utilities

  • Responsive Design

  • SVG Image Marker
  • Variable Driven
  • Project Customizable
li-img (--li-img: url('...');)
  • SVG Image Marker
  • Variable Driven
  • Project Customizable

Margin Auto System

Margin Auto Utility Classes

.ma-auto { margin: auto;        }
.mt-auto { margin-top: auto;    }
.mr-auto { margin-right: auto;  }
.mb-auto { margin-bottom: auto; }
.ml-auto { margin-left: auto;   }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.ma-tb-auto { margin: auto;        }
.mt-tb-auto { margin-top: auto;    }
.mr-tb-auto { margin-right: auto;  }
.mb-tb-auto { margin-bottom: auto; }
.ml-tb-auto { margin-left: auto;   }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.ma-mb-auto { margin: auto;        }
.mt-mb-auto { margin-top: auto;    }
.mr-mb-auto { margin-right: auto;  }
.mb-mb-auto { margin-bottom: auto; }
.ml-mb-auto { margin-left: auto;   }
}

Helper Classes

.mx-auto { margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; }
.my-auto { margin-top: auto; margin-bottom: auto; }

🟢 ma-auto utilities provide layout alignment behavior, not fixed spacing.
🟢 In ma-auto "a" represents all sides (top, right, bottom, left)
🟢 Works by consuming available free space in the layout.
🟡 Commonly used for alignment in flex and grid layouts.
🟡 Auto margins override fixed margin values on the same side
🟡 Only takes effect when free space exists (no effect in tightly packed layouts).
🟡 mx-auto centers elements horizontally when width is constrained.
🔴 Does not follow the spacing scale — avoid using for visual spacing.
🔴 May interfere with position: sticky and scrolling behavior
🟢 Base (of-*) controls how overflow is handled
🟡 Axis utilities (of-x-*, of-y-*) override specific directions
🟡 Last class wins when multiple overflow utilities are applied
🔴 Overflow values do not stack — only one applies per axis
🔴 Does not create scroll unless content exceeds container bounds

Object System

Object Fit Utility Classes

.obj-cover      { object-fit: cover;      }
.obj-contain    { object-fit: contain;    }
.obj-fill       { object-fit: fill;       }
.obj-none       { object-fit: none;       }
.obj-scale-down { object-fit: scale-down; }

🟢 controls how media is scaled and fitted inside its container
🟢 works with img, video, canvas, iframe
🟢 .obj-cover is ideal for background-like visuals
🟡 .obj-contain preserves full visibility but may introduce empty space

Object Position Utility Classes

.obj-tl { object-position: left top;      }
.obj-tc { object-position: center top;    }
.obj-tr { object-position: right top;     }
.obj-cl { object-position: left center;   }
.obj-cc { object-position: center center; }
.obj-cr { object-position: right center;  }
.obj-bl { object-position: left bottom;   }
.obj-bc { object-position: center bottom; }
.obj-br { object-position: right bottom;  }

🟢 controls which part of the media (focal point) is visible inside the container
🟢 only noticeable when media is cropped (e.g., with .obj-cover)
🟢 pairs naturally with .obj-cover
🟡 think of it as controlling which part of the image stays visible

Live Demo

Direct image vs Background image

🟢 Direct images follow normal layout flow and preserve intrinsic size.
🟢 Background images are removed from flow and behave as visual layers.
🔴 Background images cannot influence layout size or spacing.
🟡 Use direct images when the image is part of the content, and background images when it's purely decorative.

Direct image 
<img src="" alt="">
Natural flow · intrinsic size
Background like image 
<div class="ar-16x9">
  <img class="w-100p h-100p obj-cover" src="" alt="">
</div>
Detached from flow · layered · clipped

Anchoring controls focal point, not size

🟡 Anchoring affects which part of the image is visible after cropping.
🟢 bg-* utilities control object-position inside the container.
🟢 Works only with object-fit: cover behavior.
🔴 Anchoring does not change layout or image size.
🟡 Visual exaggeration (height / offset) is used here for demonstration.

Layering

🟢 Layering is controlled using pn-* (position) and z-* utilities.
🟢 enables stacking of image, overlay, and content.
🟢 Each layer should have explicit z-index for clarity.
🔴 Avoid relying on default stacking — it becomes unpredictable.
🟡 Overusing layers may increase complexity and maintenance cost.

Layered content
image · overlay · text
Explicit layering 
<div class="ar-16x9 pn-relative">

  <img class="w-100p h-100p obj-cover z-1" src="" alt="">

  <div class="inset-0 pn-absolute z-2 bg-main op-40"></div>

  <div class="pn-relative z-3 clr-white bg-g7 pn-absolute b-50px w-fit pa-xs">
    Content
  </div>

</div>
Image · overlay · content stacking

Offset System

Variables

:root {
  --t-add-p: 0%;
  --t-add-px-10: 0px;
  --t-add-px-1: 0px;
  --r-add-p: 0%;
  --r-add-px-10: 0px;
  --r-add-px-1: 0px;
  --b-add-p: 0%;
  --b-add-px-10: 0px;
  --b-add-px-1: 0px;
  --l-add-p: 0%;
  --l-add-px-10: 0px;
  --l-add-px-1: 0px;
}
.t-0 { --t-offset: 0; }
.r-0 { --r-offset: 0; }
.b-0 { --b-offset: 0; }
.l-0 { --l-offset: 0; }

.t-auto { --t-offset: auto; }
.r-auto { --r-offset: auto; }
.b-auto { --b-offset: auto; }
.l-auto { --l-offset: auto; }

Offset Engine

[class^="t-"],[class*=" t-"] { top: var(--t-offset, auto);    }
[class^="r-"],[class*=" r-"] { right: var(--r-offset, auto);  }
[class^="b-"],[class*=" b-"] { bottom: var(--b-offset, auto); }
[class^="l-"],[class*=" l-"] { left: var(--l-offset, auto);   }

🟢 Any t- / r- / b- / l- class activates the offset system
🔴 Use only one base class per axis (t / r / b / l)
🟡 Offsets require pn-relative or pn-absolute to take effect
🔴 Avoid using offset classes without positioning context
🟢 Add variables (--*-add-*) enable stackable adjustments
🟢 *-0 reset offset to zero, *-auto restore default (auto)
🟢 Use offset for positioning (visual movement)
🟢 Use margin/padding for spacing (layout separation)
🟡 Offset does not create space — it shifts element position
🟡 Acts on visual position, not layout flow

🟢 px offsets use fixed values for precise positioning
🟢 Not responsive — independent of container size
🟡 Can be combined with % offsets on different axes
🟢 Supports stackable add-* for fine control (combines large 10px + small 1px adjustments)
🟢 Negative (--*) moves in opposite direction with exact precision

Opacity System

Opacity Utility Classes

.op-0   { opacity: 0;   }
.op-10  { opacity: 0.1; }
.op-20  { opacity: 0.2; }
.op-30  { opacity: 0.3; }
.op-40  { opacity: 0.4; }
.op-50  { opacity: 0.5; }
.op-60  { opacity: 0.6; }
.op-70  { opacity: 0.7; }
.op-80  { opacity: 0.8; }
.op-90  { opacity: 0.9; }
.op-100 { opacity: 1;   }

🟢 Opacity affects the entire element including all children
🟢 Opacity transitions smoothly and pairs well with ts-* utilities
🔴 Cannot isolate only background, text, or image using opacity
🟢 Use layering (position + overlay) to control opacity precisely
🟡 Low opacity reduces readability, especially for text
🟡 Opacity affects visual stacking and contrast in layered UI
🔴 Very low opacity may reduce visibility of interactive elements

Hover Engine

[class*=" hs-op-"]:hover,
[class^="hs-op-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class^="hs-op-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" hs-op-"]:hover {
  opacity: var(--hs-op);
}

Hover Utility Classes

.hs-op-0   { --hs-op: 0;   }
.hs-op-10  { --hs-op: 0.1; }
.hs-op-20  { --hs-op: 0.2; }
.hs-op-30  { --hs-op: 0.3; }
.hs-op-40  { --hs-op: 0.4; }
.hs-op-50  { --hs-op: 0.5; }
.hs-op-60  { --hs-op: 0.6; }
.hs-op-70  { --hs-op: 0.7; }
.hs-op-80  { --hs-op: 0.8; }
.hs-op-90  { --hs-op: 0.9; }
.hs-op-100 { --hs-op: 1;   }

Child Hover Engine

.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-op-"],
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" chs-op-"] {
  opacity: var(--chs-op);
}

Child Hover Utility Classes

.chs-op-0   { --chs-op: 0;   }
.chs-op-10  { --chs-op: 0.1; }
.chs-op-20  { --chs-op: 0.2; }
.chs-op-30  { --chs-op: 0.3; }
.chs-op-40  { --chs-op: 0.4; }
.chs-op-50  { --chs-op: 0.5; }
.chs-op-60  { --chs-op: 0.6; }
.chs-op-70  { --chs-op: 0.7; }
.chs-op-80  { --chs-op: 0.8; }
.chs-op-90  { --chs-op: 0.9; }
.chs-op-100 { --chs-op: 1;   }
🟢 Base (op-*) sets default opacity
🟢 Parent (chs-op-*) applies on parent hover
🟢 Self (hs-op-*) applies on hover and overrides parent
🟡 Last class wins when conflicts occur
🟡 Opacity transitions smoothly and pairs well with ts-* utilities
🔴 hs-op-* overrides chs-op-* when both are active
🔴 Opacity affects entire element including children
🔴 Values do not stack — only one opacity applies

Live Demo

Opacity Levels (.op-*)

Basic Opacity Levels
op-100
op-70
op-40
op-10

🟢 Fades entire element (bg + text + children)
🔴 Cannot isolate only background when using opacity on parent

Text Only Opacity

op-100

op-70

op-40

op-10

🟢 Apply opacity directly to text
🟡 Do not use on large text blocks — readability drops

Image Opacity

🟢 Apply opacity to image directly
🔴 Avoid applying opacity on parent unless whole card should fade

Background Overlay (Isolated Opacity)

No overlay

bg-acnt + op-40

🔴 Do not use container opacity for this case
🟢 Applying opacity on container fades EVERYTHING
🟢 Use overlay layering for controlled opacity
🟢 Separate layers if only background should fade

Self Hover (.hs-op-*)

Basic Opacity Levels
Normal → hs-op-30
op-70 → hs-op-100
Text Only Opacity

op-100 → hs-op-30

op-30 → hs-op-100

Image Opacity

🟢 hs-op-* changes opacity only when you hover that element
🟡 Works on anything: box(fades entire box), text(fades only text), image(fades only image)
🔴 Opacity affects the whole element(background + text + children will all fade together)
🔴 You cannot fade only background using this — use overlay for that

Parent Hover (.chs-parent .chs-op-*)

Background Overlay (Isolated Opacity)

bg-main + op-0
chs-op-40

bg-acnt + op-40
chs-op-80

bg-sub + op-10
chs-op-100

🟢 chs-parent lets you trigger hover by hovering the container instead of the element itself
🟢 chs-op-* changes opacity of inner elements when the parent is hovered
🟡 Needed when you cannot hover the actual element (like overlay placed above image)
🟡 Useful for layered UI such as image + overlay + text working together
🔴 hs-op-* does not work here because the overlay is not directly hoverable
🔴 Opacity affects the entire overlay layer, not parts inside it

Order System

Order Utility Classes

.ord-0  { order: 0;  }
.ord-1  { order: 1;  }
.ord-2  { order: 2;  }
.ord-3  { order: 3;  }
.ord-4  { order: 4;  }
.ord-5  { order: 5;  }
.ord-6  { order: 6;  }
.ord-7  { order: 7;  }
.ord-8  { order: 8;  }
.ord-9  { order: 9;  }
.ord-10 { order: 10; }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.ord-tb-0  { order: 0;  }
.ord-tb-1  { order: 1;  }
.ord-tb-2  { order: 2;  }
.ord-tb-3  { order: 3;  }
.ord-tb-4  { order: 4;  }
.ord-tb-5  { order: 5;  }
.ord-tb-6  { order: 6;  }
.ord-tb-7  { order: 7;  }
.ord-tb-8  { order: 8;  }

.ord-mb-4  { order: 4;  }
.ord-mb-5  { order: 5;  }
.ord-mb-6  { order: 6;  }
.ord-mb-7  { order: 7;  }
.ord-mb-8  { order: 8;  }
.ord-mb-9  { order: 9;  }
.ord-mb-10 { order: 10; }
}

🟢 ord-* utilities control the visual order of flex and grid items.
🟢 Higher values appear later in the layout flow.
🟢 Useful for responsive content reordering without changing HTML structure.
🟡 Items with the same order value follow normal document order.
🔴 Order changes visual layout only — it does not change DOM order.
🔴 Avoid excessive reordering as it may reduce accessibility and maintainability.

Live Demo

Visual Order vs HTML Order

Item A
Item B
Item C
Default order 
<div>Item A</div>
<div>Item B</div>
<div>Item C</div>
Visual order follows HTML order
Item A (ord-3)
Item B (ord-1)
Item C (ord-2)
Custom order 
<div class="ord-3">Item A</div>
<div class="ord-1">Item B</div>
<div class="ord-2">Item C</div>
Visual order becomes B → C → A

🟢 This simulation displays desktop, tablet, and mobile results simultaneously for learning purposes.
🟢 In real usage, a single layout automatically changes order using ord-tb-* and ord-mb-* utilities.
🔴 Do not create separate layouts per breakpoint in production.

Overflow System

Overflow Utility Classes

.of-auto     { overflow: auto;     }
.of-x-auto   { overflow-x: auto;   }
.of-y-auto   { overflow-y: auto;   }
.of-hidden   { overflow: hidden;   }
.of-x-hidden { overflow-x: hidden; }
.of-y-hidden { overflow-y: hidden; }
.of-scroll   { overflow: scroll;   }
.of-x-scroll { overflow-x: scroll; }
.of-y-scroll { overflow-y: scroll; }
.of-clip     { overflow: clip;     }
.of-x-clip   { overflow-x: clip;   }
.of-y-clip   { overflow-y: clip;   }
.of-visible  { overflow: visible;  }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.of-tb-auto     { overflow: auto;     }
.of-tb-x-auto   { overflow-x: auto;   }
.of-tb-y-auto   { overflow-y: auto;   }
.of-tb-hidden   { overflow: hidden;   }
.of-tb-x-hidden { overflow-x: hidden; }
.of-tb-y-hidden { overflow-y: hidden; }
.of-tb-scroll   { overflow: scroll;   }
.of-tb-x-scroll { overflow-x: scroll; }
.of-tb-y-scroll { overflow-y: scroll; }
.of-tb-clip     { overflow: clip;     }
.of-tb-x-clip   { overflow-x: clip;   }
.of-tb-y-clip   { overflow-y: clip;   }
.of-tb-visible  { overflow: visible;  }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.of-mb-auto     { overflow: auto;     }
.of-mb-x-auto   { overflow-x: auto;   }
.of-mb-y-auto   { overflow-y: auto;   }
.of-mb-hidden   { overflow: hidden;   }
.of-mb-x-hidden { overflow-x: hidden; }
.of-mb-y-hidden { overflow-y: hidden; }
.of-mb-scroll   { overflow: scroll;   }
.of-mb-x-scroll { overflow-x: scroll; }
.of-mb-y-scroll { overflow-y: scroll; }
.of-mb-clip     { overflow: clip;     }
.of-mb-x-clip   { overflow-x: clip;   }
.of-mb-y-clip   { overflow-y: clip;   }
.of-mb-visible  { overflow: visible;  }
}

🟢 of-* utilities control how content behaves when it exceeds container bounds.
🟢 of-hidden clips overflow, of-auto adds scroll only when needed, of-scroll always shows scrollbars.
🟢 Axis utilities (of-x-*, of-y-*) provide directional control.
🟡 Often used with fixed height / width or aspect ratio (ar-*) containers.
🟡 Responsive variants (of-tb-*, of-mb-*) help manage overflow across devices.
🔴 Incorrect overflow usage can break layouts (e.g. disabling sticky or clipping content unexpectedly).

Pointer Events System

Pointer Events Utility Classes

.pointer-auto { pointer-events: auto; }
.pointer-none { pointer-events: none; }

🟢 pointer-auto enables normal mouse, touch and pointer interactions on an element
🟢 pointer-none disables mouse, touch and pointer interactions on an element
🟢 Useful for overlays, decorative elements, disabled UI states and click-through effects
🟡 Child elements inherit pointer behavior unless explicitly overridden
🟡 Elements with pointer-none remain visible but cannot receive clicks, hover events or touch interactions
🔴 Pointer Events utilities affect pointer interaction only and do not control keyboard focus or accessibility behavior

pointer-none

Position System

Position Utility Classes

.pn-static   { position: static;   }
.pn-relative { position: relative; }
.pn-absolute { position: absolute; }
.pn-fixed    { position: fixed;    }
.pn-sticky   { position: sticky;   }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.pn-tb-static   { position: static;   }
.pn-tb-relative { position: relative; }
.pn-tb-absolute { position: absolute; }
.pn-tb-fixed    { position: fixed;    }
.pn-tb-sticky   { position: sticky;   }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.pn-mb-static   { position: static;   }
.pn-mb-relative { position: relative; }
.pn-mb-absolute { position: absolute; }
.pn-mb-fixed    { position: fixed;    }
.pn-mb-sticky   { position: sticky;   }
}

Helper Classes

.pn-center {
  top: 50%;
  left: 50%;
  transform: translate(-50%, -50%);
}

🟢 pn-* utilities (static, relative, absolute, fixed, sticky) define positioning behavior, not spacing.
🟢 Works by placing elements relative to a parent container or viewport using positioning context.
🟢 Supports alignment patterns (e.g., pn-center) for precise placement.
🟡 Requires proper setup (e.g., pn-relative parent for pn-absolute, or viewport for pn-fixed).
🟡 Responsive variants (pn-tb-*, pn-mb-*) allow position changes across devices.
🔴 Some position values (relative, absolute, fixed, sticky) can create stacking contexts
🔴 Sticky requires a scroll container and may not work inside overflow-hidden parents
🔴 Does not follow the spacing scale — avoid using positioning for layout spacing; use margin/padding instead.

Live Demo

Static vs Relative (Visual Switch)

Static

Relative

Viewport reference (fallback anchor)
Parent: static
absolute

🟢 pn-static and pn-relative appear identical visually — both follow normal document flow.
🟢 The difference is behavioral: only pn-relative creates a positioning context.
🟢 pn-absolute children anchor to the nearest pn-relative parent.
🔴 With pn-static, absolute elements fall back to the nearest positioned ancestor (or viewport).
🟡 This behavior cannot be understood visually without an absolute child reacting to it.
🔴 Does not affect spacing — use margin/padding for layout spacing, not positioning.

Absolute vs Fixed (Visual Switch)

Absolute

Fixed

Viewport reference
Parent: relative
absolute

🟢 pn-absolute positions relative to a parent container.
🟢 pn-fixed positions relative to the viewport.
🟡 Scroll to observe: absolute moves, fixed stays pinned.
🔴 Fixed ignores parent layout completely.

Relative vs Sticky (Real Layout)

Relative

Sticky

Navbar - position: relative

relative

Sidebar block
sticks when enabled

Scroll content
Scroll content

🟢 pn-relative scrolls normally with content and does not stick.
🟢 pn-sticky behaves like relative until it reaches an offset (e.g. t-0), then sticks in place.
🟢 Works for both navbar (top) and sidebar (within layout) in this demo.
🟡 Sticky only works inside its scroll container and requires a defined offset.
🟡 Parent layout and spacing (e.g. navbar height) must be considered to avoid overlap.
🔴 overflow: hidden / auto / scroll on parent elements can break or limit sticky behavior.
🔴 Sticky = relative → then locks during scroll, not a global fixed position.

Resize System

Resize Utility Classes

.resize-none { resize: none;       }
.resize-both { resize: both;       }
.resize-x    { resize: horizontal; }
.resize-y    { resize: vertical;   }

🟢 resize-none prevents users from resizing an element
🟢 resize-both allows horizontal and vertical resizing
🟢 resize-x allows horizontal resizing only
🟢 resize-y allows vertical resizing only
🟢 Commonly used with textareas, editors, panels and custom content regions
🟡 Resize handles may appear differently across browsers and operating systems
🟡 Resize behavior is most effective when combined with overflow utilities
🔴 Resize utilities affect user resizing behavior only and do not control element dimensions directly

Radius System

Variables

:root {
  --ra-xxs: 5px;
  --ra-xs : 10px;
  --ra-sm : 15px;
  --ra-md : 20px;
  --ra-lg : 25px;
  --ra-xl : 30px;
  --ra-xxl: 100px;
  --ra-max: 9999px;
}

Helper Classes

.ra-max  { border-radius: var(--ra-max);              }
.rtl-max { border-top-left-radius: var(--ra-max);     }
.rtr-max { border-top-right-radius: var(--ra-max);    }
.rbr-max { border-bottom-right-radius: var(--ra-max); }
.rbl-max { border-bottom-left-radius: var(--ra-max);  }

🟢 ra-max is a helper for fully rounded shapes (pills, circles)
🟡 It is not included in hr/chr transitions because it reaches the visual radius limit instantly
🟡 Each corner is controlled independently — no fallback or shared base is applied
🟡 Due to geometric limits, values beyond the element’s max radius are visually identical (no smooth interpolation)
🟡 Use ra-xxl as the largest animatable radius and ra-max only as a final/static shape

Radius Utility Classes

.ra-xxs { border-radius: var(--ra-xxs); }
.ra-xs  { border-radius: var(--ra-xs);  }
.ra-sm  { border-radius: var(--ra-sm);  }
.ra-md  { border-radius: var(--ra-md);  }

.rtl-0   { border-top-left-radius: 0;             }
.rtr-xxs { border-top-right-radius: var(--ra-xxs); }
.rtr-xs  { border-top-right-radius: var(--ra-xs);  }
.rtr-sm  { border-top-right-radius: var(--ra-sm);  }
.rtr-md  { border-top-right-radius: var(--ra-md);  }
.rtr-lg  { border-top-right-radius: var(--ra-lg);  }

.rbl-xl  { border-bottom-left-radius: var(--ra-xl);  }
.rbl-xxl { border-bottom-left-radius: var(--ra-xxl); }
.rbl-0   { border-bottom-left-radius: 0;             }

🟢 ra-* defines corner rounding using a consistent scale (xxs → xxl)
🟢 Directional utilities (rtl / rtr / rbl / rbr) allow independent control of each corner
🟢 Radius uses CSS variables to keep values consistent and easily adjustable
🟢 hs-ra-* enables smooth radius transitions on self hover
🟢 chs-ra-* applies radius changes when the parent is hovered
🟡 Parent sets a base shape, self hover refines it per element
🟡 Use subtle radius changes for better visual transitions
🔴 Radius affects visual shape only and does not change layout
🔴 Avoid extreme jumps (e.g., ra-0 → ra-max) as they may feel abrupt

🟢 Base utilities (ra-*) define default shape
🟢 Parent utilities (chs-*) apply on parent hover
🟢 Self utilities (hs-*) apply on element hover and override parent
🔴 Directional radius overrides full radius when both are applied
🟡 Last class wins when multiple utilities target the same property
🔴 Radius values do not stack or blend — only one applies at a time

🟢 ra-* applies radius to all corners
🟢 rtl, rtr, rbr, rbl control individual corners
🟢 Multiple directional utilities can be combined to create custom shapes
🟡 Directional utilities allow asymmetric shapes
🔴 Radius affects only shape, not layout

🟢 Radius uses a semantic scale (xxs → xxl)
🟢 ra-max creates fully rounded shapes for pills, circles, and avatars
🔴 ra-max is not part of scale — use intentionally
🟢 Larger values create softer shapes
🟡 Choose based on visual feel, not exact size
🔴 Avoid mixing too many radius levels in one component

🟢 Smooth transitions enhance interaction
🟡 Use subtle changes for better UX
🔴 Avoid extreme jumps for consistency

Parent & Self Hover (.chs-parent .chs-ra-* .hs-ra-*)

Parent Hover Area (hover this box)

chs-parent

Normal
chs-ra-lg
ra-md
chs-ra-sm
ra-sm
chs-ra-xxl

🟢 Hover this container to apply shape changes
🟢 chs-ra-* updates radius for all items
🟡 All cards change together (group behavior)
🔴 Requires chs-parent to activate
🔴 Radius affects shape only (no layout change)

Parent sets, Self refines

chs-parent

Normal
chs-ra-lg
hs-ra-sm
ra-md
chs-ra-xxl
hs-ra-0
ra-sm
chs-ra-xs
hs-ra-0

🟢 chs-ra-* applies radius on parent hover (group shape)
🟢 hs-ra-* applies radius on self hover (override)
🟡 Parent sets base shape, self refines it per item
🔴 hs-* overrides chs-* when both are active
🔴 Only one radius value applies (no stacking)

Radius Transition with ra-max (Limitation Demo)

ra-max
hs-ra-sm
ra-max
hs-ra-sm
ra-xxl
hs-ra-sm
ra-xxl
hs-ra-sm

🟢 hs-ra-* updates the radius on hover using smooth transitions
🟡 When starting from ra-max, the visible radius is already at its maximum limit
🟡 On hover, the transition appears abrupt because most intermediate values are visually identical
🟡 The effect is more noticeable on smaller elements (lower max-radius cap)
🔴 Why this happens: border-radius is constrained by element size (min(width/2, height/2))
🔴 Values beyond this limit (like ra-max) collapse visually, reducing transition range
🟢 Best Practice: use ra-xxl for smooth animations
🟢 use ra-max only for final/static shapes (pills, circles)

Stress and Limitation Test

Parent/Self Hover (Layerd)

chs-parent

Normal | chs-ra-lg | hs-ra-0
Normal | chs-ra-xxl | hs-ra-0
ra-xxl | chs-ra-xl | hs-ra-sm
Parent/Self Hover (Corners)

chs-parent

Normal | chs-rtr-lg | hs-rtl-xxl
Normal | chs-rbr-xxl | hs-rbr-0
ra-xxl | chs-rbl-xl | hs-rtr-sm | hs-rbr-sm

🟢 Layering Test: chs-parent > chs-ra-lg > hs-ra-0
🟢 Nested Parent Test: chs-parent > chs-ra-lg > chs-ra-xxl > chs-ra-xl.hs-ra-sm
🟢 Mixed Direction + State Test: hs-rtl-xxl + chs-rtr-lg
🟢 Multi-State Combination Test: chs-rbl-xl + hs-rtr-sm + hs-rbr-sm
🟡 Deep Nesting: chs-parent > chs-parent > chs-parent (avoid for readability)

Shadow System

Variables

:root {
  --sw-xxs: 0 2px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.10);
  --sw-xs : 0 3px 10px rgba(0,0,0,0.11);
  --sw-sm : 0 4px 12px rgba(0,0,0,0.12);
  --sw-md : 0 6px 16px rgba(0,0,0,0.13);
  --sw-lg : 0 8px 20px rgba(0,0,0,0.14);
  --sw-xl : 0 12px 26px rgba(0,0,0,0.15);
  --sw-xxl: 0 16px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.16);
  --sw-0  : 0 0 0 rgba(0,0,0,0);
}

Shadow Utility Classes

.sw-xxs { box-shadow: var(--sw-xxs); }
.sw-xs  { box-shadow: var(--sw-xs);  }
.sw-sm  { box-shadow: var(--sw-sm);  }
.sw-md  { box-shadow: var(--sw-md);  }
.sw-lg  { box-shadow: var(--sw-lg);  }
.sw-xl  { box-shadow: var(--sw-xl);  }
.sw-xxl { box-shadow: var(--sw-xxl); }
.sw-0   { box-shadow: var(--sw-0);   }

Hover Engine

[class*=" hs-sw-"]:hover,
[class^="hs-sw-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class^="hs-sw-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" hs-sw-"]:hover {
  box-shadow: var(--hs-sw);
}

Hover Utility Classes

.hs-sw-xxs { --hs-sw: var(--sw-xxs); }
.hs-sw-xs  { --hs-sw: var(--sw-xs);  }
.hs-sw-sm  { --hs-sw: var(--sw-sm);  }
.hs-sw-md  { --hs-sw: var(--sw-md);  }
.hs-sw-lg  { --hs-sw: var(--sw-lg);  }
.hs-sw-xl  { --hs-sw: var(--sw-xl);  }
.hs-sw-xxl { --hs-sw: var(--sw-xxl); }
.hs-sw-0   { --hs-sw: var(--sw-0);   }

Child Hover Engine

.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-sw-"],
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" chs-sw-"] {
  box-shadow: var(--chs-sw);
}

Child Hover Utility Classes

.chs-sw-xxs { --chs-sw: var(--sw-xxs); }
.chs-sw-xs  { --chs-sw: var(--sw-xs);  }
.chs-sw-sm  { --chs-sw: var(--sw-sm);  }
.chs-sw-md  { --chs-sw: var(--sw-md);  }
.chs-sw-lg  { --chs-sw: var(--sw-lg);  }
.chs-sw-xl  { --chs-sw: var(--sw-xl);  }
.chs-sw-xxl { --chs-sw: var(--sw-xxl); }
.chs-sw-0   { --chs-sw: var(--sw-0);   }

🟢 sw-* utilities apply elevation using box-shadow
🟡 Shadow is controlled as a single value — no directional or partial shadow control is provided
🟢 Scale ranges from subtle (xxs) to strong (xxl)
🔴 Shadows do not affect layout, size, or spacing
🟡 Use sparingly — excessive shadows create visual noise
🟡 Depth should reflect importance, not decoration

🟢 chs-parent enables group-level interaction (container hover)
🟢 chs-sw-* defines shadow for elements when the parent is hovered
🟢 Useful for coordinated UI behavior (cards, lists, grouped elements)
🟡 Best used to create a unified hover experience across multiple elements
🔴 Applies the same trigger to all matching children — individual control happens via chs-*

Parent sets, Self refines

chs-parent

sw-xxs

chs-sw-md

sw-xxs
chs-sw-sm
hs-sw-0

🟢 chs-sw-* defines shadow on parent hover (group baseline)
🟢 hs-sw-* defines shadow on self hover (element override)
🟡 Shadow is controlled through a single variable (--sw) — all interactions modify this
🟢 Both can work together — self hover always overrides parent hover when active
🔴 Shadow is a single-value property — no stacking, only one value is applied at a time

Space System

Variables

:root {--space-unit: 8px;}

@media (max-width: 990px) {
  :root {--space-unit: 6px;}
}

@media (max-width: 770px) {
  :root {--space-unit: 4px;}
}
:root {
--sp-xxs: calc(var(--space-unit) * 1);
--sp-xs : calc(var(--space-unit) * 2);
--sp-sm : calc(var(--space-unit) * 3);
--sp-md : calc(var(--space-unit) * 4);
--sp-lg : calc(var(--space-unit) * 6);
--sp-xl : calc(var(--space-unit) * 8);
--sp-xxl: calc(var(--space-unit) * 12);
}

/* 8px  / 6px  / 4px  */
/* 16px / 12px / 8px  */
/* 24px / 18px / 12px */
/* 32px / 24px / 16px */
/* 48px / 36px / 24px */
/* 64px / 48px / 32px */
/* 96px / 72px / 48px */

🟢 Spacing is based on a multiplier scale applied to --space-unit
🟢 Scale follows: ×1, ×2, ×3, ×4, ×6, ×8, ×12
🟢 Each step (xxs → xxl) increases spacing consistently across the system

🟢 Defines a scalable spacing system using CSS variables
🟢 Spacing values are derived from --space-unit and a fixed multiplier scale
🟡 Changing --space-unit updates all spacing globally — use with awareness
🟢 Spacing scale (xxs → xxl) ensures consistent spacing across components
🟢 pa-*, ma-*, gap-* and stack-* utilities apply spacing uniformly
🟢 Directional variants (x, y, t, r, b, l) control spacing on specific sides
🟢 0 utilities (pa-0, ma-0, gap-0, stack-0) reset spacing when needed
🟡 They act as strong overrides but still follow normal CSS specificity rules
🔴 May be overridden by more specific selectors or later declarations

🟡 In demos, spacing is calculated using inline calc(var(--space-unit) * n) with same relationship (×1, ×2, ×3, ×4, ×6, ×8, ×12)
🟢 In real usage, always rely on spacing tokens (--sp-*) for consistency
🟡 Demos override --space-unit locally to show Desktop, Tablet, and Mobile side-by-side
🟢 In actual usage, spacing is controlled centrally via :root and media queries

Text System

Text Align Utility Classes

.txt-left    { text-align: left;     }
.txt-center  { text-align: center;   }
.txt-right   { text-align: right;    }
.txt-start   { text-align: start;    }
.txt-end     { text-align: end;      }
.txt-justify { text-align: justify;  }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.txt-tb-left    { text-align: left;     }
.txt-tb-center  { text-align: center;   }
.txt-tb-right   { text-align: right;    }
.txt-tb-start   { text-align: start;    }
.txt-tb-end     { text-align: end;      }
.txt-tb-justify { text-align: justify;  }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.txt-mb-left    { text-align: left;     }
.txt-mb-center  { text-align: center;   }
.txt-mb-right   { text-align: right;    }
.txt-mb-start   { text-align: start;    }
.txt-mb-end     { text-align: end;      }
.txt-mb-justify { text-align: justify;  }
}

🟢 txt-left aligns text to the left edge of its container
🟢 txt-center centers text horizontally within its container
🟢 txt-right aligns text to the right edge of its container
🟢 txt-start aligns text to the natural reading start (left in LTR, right in RTL)
🟢 txt-end aligns text to the natural reading end (right in LTR, left in RTL)
🟡 txt-justify stretches lines to fill available width and works best for longer paragraphs
🔴 Text alignment affects content placement only and does not control Flex or Grid alignment

Text Vertical Align Utility Classes

.txt-top    { vertical-align: top;    }
.txt-middle { vertical-align: middle; }
.txt-bottom { vertical-align: bottom; }

🟢 txt-top, txt-middle and txt-bottom control the vertical alignment of the element they are applied to.
🟢 Commonly used with icons, images, badges, inline-block elements and table cells.
🟡 The surrounding text does not move — the aligned element shifts relative to the current text line.
🟡 Effects are most noticeable when elements have different sizes.
🟢 The demo intentionally uses a larger icon beside smaller text to exaggerate the behavior.
🔴 Vertical alignment does not affect Flex or Grid layouts.

Vertical Align Live Demo

txt-top ⬜ Sample Text
txt-middle ⬜ Sample Text
txt-bottom ⬜ Sample Text

Text Direction Utility Classes

.txt-ltr { direction: ltr; }
.txt-rtl { direction: rtl; }

🟢 txt-ltr displays content from left to right, commonly used for English and most western languages
🟢 txt-rtl displays content from right to left, commonly used for Arabic and Hebrew
🟢 Direction directly affects logical utilities such as txt-start and txt-end
🟡 Apply direction to content containers rather than individual words whenever possible
🔴 Direction changes reading flow only and does not affect Flex or Grid layout behavior

Direction Live Demo

txt-rtl

Text Transform Utility Classes

.txt-uppercase      { text-transform: uppercase;  }
.txt-lowercase      { text-transform: lowercase;  }
.txt-capitalize     { text-transform: capitalize; }
.txt-transform-none { text-transform: none;       }

🟢 txt-uppercase converts all letters to uppercase characters
🟢 txt-lowercase converts all letters to lowercase characters
🟢 txt-capitalize converts the first letter of each word to uppercase
🟢 txt-transform-none displays text using its original casing
🟢 Useful for labels, buttons, badges, navigation items and consistent UI presentation
🔴 Text transformation changes appearance only and does not modify the underlying text value

Text Decoration Utility Classes

.txt-underline       { text-decoration: underline;     }
.txt-overline        { text-decoration: overline;      }
.txt-line-through    { text-decoration: line-through;  }
.txt-decoration-none { text-decoration: none;          }

🟢 txt-underline draws a line beneath the text and is commonly used for links and emphasis
🟢 txt-overline draws a line above the text for special visual emphasis
🟢 txt-line-through draws a line through the text and is often used for discounts or completed items
🟢 txt-decoration-none removes decoration lines from decorated text such as links
🟡 Decoration utilities are mutually exclusive — the last utility wins
🔴 Decoration changes appearance only and does not affect layout or text behavior

Text Style Utility Classes

.txt-italic     { font-style: italic;  }
.txt-style-none { font-style: normal;  }

🟢 txt-italic displays text using an italic font style and is commonly used for quotes, references and emphasis
🟢 txt-style-none removes italic styling and restores normal text style
🟡 Font Style utilities are intentionally grouped within the Text System because they affect text presentation
🔴 Font Style utilities affect text appearance only and do not modify font family, font weight or layout behavior

Text Wrap Utility Classes

.txt-nowrap { 
  white-space: nowrap; 
}

.txt-break {
  overflow-wrap: break-word;
  word-break: break-word;
}

🟢 txt-nowrap forces text to remain on a single line even when space runs out
🟢 txt-break allows long words, URLs and unbroken strings to split across multiple lines when necessary
🟡 txt-break is useful for user-generated content, links and dynamic data
🔴 Wrap utilities control text flow only and do not resize or reposition elements

Text Ellipsis Engine

[class^="ellipsis-"], [class*=" ellipsis-"] {
  overflow:hidden;
  display:-webkit-box;
  -webkit-box-orient:vertical;
  text-overflow:ellipsis;
}

Text Ellipsis Utility Classes

.ellipsis-1 { -webkit-line-clamp:1; }
.ellipsis-2 { -webkit-line-clamp:2; }
.ellipsis-3 { -webkit-line-clamp:3; }
.ellipsis-4 { -webkit-line-clamp:4; }
.ellipsis-5 { -webkit-line-clamp:5; }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.ellipsis-tb-1 { -webkit-line-clamp:1; }
.ellipsis-tb-2 { -webkit-line-clamp:2; }
.ellipsis-tb-3 { -webkit-line-clamp:3; }
.ellipsis-tb-4 { -webkit-line-clamp:4; }
.ellipsis-tb-5 { -webkit-line-clamp:5; }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.ellipsis-mb-1 { -webkit-line-clamp:1; }
.ellipsis-mb-2 { -webkit-line-clamp:2; }
.ellipsis-mb-3 { -webkit-line-clamp:3; }
.ellipsis-mb-4 { -webkit-line-clamp:4; }
.ellipsis-mb-5 { -webkit-line-clamp:5; }
}

🟢 ellipsis-1 limits content to a single line and displays an ellipsis (...) when text overflows
🟢 ellipsis-2, ellipsis-3, ellipsis-4 and ellipsis-5 limit content to a fixed number of visible lines
🟢 Useful for cards, product grids, article previews, tables and dynamic content layouts
🟢 Helps maintain consistent component heights when content length varies
🟡 Requires constrained width to produce visible truncation
🔴 Hidden content is not removed — only visually clipped from display

Transform System

Transform Engine

[class^="tt-"],         [class*=" tt-"],
[class^="tr-"],         [class*=" tr-"],
[class^="tb-"],         [class*=" tb-"],
[class^="tl-"],         [class*=" tl-"],
[class^="scale-"],      [class*=" scale-"],
[class^="skewx-"],      [class*=" skewx-"],
[class^="skewy-"],      [class*=" skewy-"],
[class^="rotate-"],     [class*=" rotate-"],

[class^="hs-tt-"],      [class*=" hs-tt-"],
[class^="hs-tr-"],      [class*=" hs-tr-"],
[class^="hs-tb-"],      [class*=" hs-tb-"],
[class^="hs-tl-"],      [class*=" hs-tl-"],
[class^="hs-scale-"],   [class*=" hs-scale-"],
[class^="hs-skewx-"],   [class*=" hs-skewx-"],
[class^="hs-skewy-"],   [class*=" hs-skewy-"],
[class^="hs-rotate-"],  [class*=" hs-rotate-"],

[class^="chs-tt-"],     [class*=" chs-tt-"],
[class^="chs-tr-"],     [class*=" chs-tr-"],
[class^="chs-tb-"],     [class*=" chs-tb-"],
[class^="chs-tl-"],     [class*=" chs-tl-"],
[class^="chs-scale-"],  [class*=" chs-scale-"], 
[class^="chs-skewx-"],  [class*=" chs-skewx-"],
[class^="chs-skewy-"],  [class*=" chs-skewy-"],
[class^="chs-rotate-"], [class*=" chs-rotate-"]{
  transform:
    rotate(var(--rotate-value, 0deg))
    scale(var(--scale-value, 1))
    skewX(var(--skewx-value, 0deg))
    skewY(var(--skewy-value, 0deg))
    translateX(calc(var(--translate-right, 0px) - var(--translate-left, 0px)))
    translateY(calc(var(--translate-bottom, 0px) - var(--translate-top, 0px)));
}

Origin Utility Classes

.origin-center { transform-origin: center;       }
.origin-top    { transform-origin: top;          }
.origin-right  { transform-origin: right;        }
.origin-bottom { transform-origin: bottom;       }
.origin-left   { transform-origin: left;         }
.origin-tl     { transform-origin: top left;     }
.origin-tr     { transform-origin: top right;    }
.origin-br     { transform-origin: bottom right; }
.origin-bl     { transform-origin: bottom left;  }

🟢 origin-* defines the pivot point used by Scale, Rotate and Skew
🟢 Supports center, edge and corner origins
🟢 Controls where Scale expands from and where Rotate and Skew pivots from
🟡 Has no visual effect until used with Scale, Rotate or Skew
🟡 Transform order is fixed: Rotate → Scale → SkewX → SkewY → TranslateX → TranslateY
🔴 Origin utilities do not combine — the last origin utility wins
🔴 Multiple Scale utilities do not combine
🔴 Use origin-tl, origin-tr, origin-bl or origin-br for corner origins

🟢 scale-*p scales rendered size using percentages
🟢 scale-100p represents the original size (100%)
🟢 Supports values from 0%–200%
🟢 Supports additive precision through scale-add-*p
🟢 Example: scale-110p + scale-add-3p = 113%
🟡 Visual scaling only — does not affect layout
🔴 Use Width and Height utilities for physical sizing
🟢 hs-scale-*p applies Scale on self hover
🟢 Supports additive precision through hs-scale-add-*p
🟢 Ideal for buttons, cards and micro-interactions
🟢 chs-scale-*p applies Scale from a chs-parent hover
🟢 Supports additive precision through chs-scale-add-*p
🟢 Useful for group interactions and animated layouts
🔴 Multiple Scale utilities do not combine — the last Scale utility wins

🟢 Can be combined with Translate, Scale and Rotate

🟢 chs-skewx-* / chs-skewy-* apply Skew from a chs-parent hover
🟡 Skew changes visual shape only — does not affect layout

  --tr-add-px: 0px;
  --tb-add-p: 0%;
  --tb-add-px: 0px;
  --tl-add-p: 0%;
  --tl-add-px: 0px;

  --hs-tt-add-p: 0%;
  --hs-tt-add-px: 0px;
  --hs-tr-add-p: 0%;
  --hs-tr-add-px: 0px;
  --hs-tb-add-p: 0%;
  --hs-tb-add-px: 0px;
  --hs-tl-add-p: 0%;
  --hs-tl-add-px: 0px;

  --chs-tt-add-px: 0px;

  --chs-tr-add-px: 0px;
  --chs-tb-add-p: 0%;
  --chs-tb-add-px: 0px;
  --chs-tl-add-p: 0%;
  --chs-tl-add-px: 0px;

.chs-tl-30px  { --chs-tl: calc(30px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-40px  { --chs-tl: calc(40px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-50px  { --chs-tl: calc(50px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-60px  { --chs-tl: calc(60px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-70px  { --chs-tl: calc(70px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-80px  { --chs-tl: calc(80px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-90px  { --chs-tl: calc(90px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-100px { --chs-tl: calc(100px + var(--chs-tl-add-px)); }
.chs-tl-add-1px { --chs-tl-add-px: 1px; }
.chs-tl-add-2px { --chs-tl-add-px: 2px; }
.chs-tl-add-3px { --chs-tl-add-px: 3px; }
.chs-tl-add-4px { --chs-tl-add-px: 4px; }
.chs-tl-add-5px { --chs-tl-add-px: 5px; }
.chs-tl-add-6px { --chs-tl-add-px: 6px; }
.chs-tl-add-7px { --chs-tl-add-px: 7px; }
.chs-tl-add-8px { --chs-tl-add-px: 8px; }
.chs-tl-add-9px { --chs-tl-add-px: 9px; }

🟢 tt-* up, tr-* right, tb-* down, tl-* left
🟢 Supports px and % values
🟢 Directions can be combined (e.g. tt-* + tr-*)
🟢 Opposite directions replace negative utilities
🟡 Visual movement only — does not affect layout
🔴 Use Offset utilities (t-* r-* b-* l-*) for positioning
🟢 hs-tt-*, hs-tr-*, hs-tb-*, hs-tl-* apply Translate on self hover
🟢 chs-tt-*, chs-tr-*, chs-tb-*, chs-tl-* apply Translate from a chs-parent hover
🟢 Supports px, %, additive precision and multi-direction movement

Transition System

Transition Engine

[class^="ts-"], [class*=" ts-"] {
  transition-property:        var(--ts-property, all);
  transition-duration:        var(--ts-duration, 0.2s);
  transition-timing-function: var(--ts-ease, ease-in-out);
  transition-delay:           var(--ts-delay, 0s);
}

Transition Utility Classes

.ts-all       { --ts-property: all;              }
.ts-opacity   { --ts-property: opacity;          }
.ts-transform { --ts-property: transform;        }
.ts-color     { --ts-property: color;            }
.ts-bg-color  { --ts-property: background-color; }
.ts-shadow    { --ts-property: box-shadow;       }
.ts-filter    { --ts-property: filter;           }
.ts-0  { --ts-duration: 0s; }
.ts-1  { --ts-duration: 0.1s; }
.ts-2  { --ts-duration: 0.2s; }
.ts-3  { --ts-duration: 0.3s; }
.ts-4  { --ts-duration: 0.4s; }
.ts-5  { --ts-duration: 0.5s; }
.ts-6  { --ts-duration: 0.6s; }
.ts-7  { --ts-duration: 0.7s; }
.ts-8  { --ts-duration: 0.8s; }
.ts-9  { --ts-duration: 0.9s; }
.ts-10 { --ts-duration: 1s;   }
.ts-linear      { --ts-ease: linear;                            }
.ts-ease        { --ts-ease: ease;                              }
.ts-ease-in     { --ts-ease: ease-in;                           }
.ts-ease-out    { --ts-ease: ease-out;                          }
.ts-ease-in-out { --ts-ease: ease-in-out;                       }
.ts-smooth      { --ts-ease: cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1);      }
.ts-snappy      { --ts-ease: cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 1, 1);        }
.ts-bounce      { --ts-ease: cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1); }
.ts-step-start  { --ts-ease: steps(1, start);                   }
.ts-step-end    { --ts-ease: steps(1, end);                     }
.ts-d0  { --ts-delay: 0s;  }
.ts-d1  { --ts-delay: 0.1s;  }
.ts-d2  { --ts-delay: 0.2s;  }
.ts-d3  { --ts-delay: 0.3s;  }
.ts-d4  { --ts-delay: 0.4s;  }
.ts-d5  { --ts-delay: 0.5s;  }
.ts-d6  { --ts-delay: 0.6s;  }
.ts-d7  { --ts-delay: 0.7s;  }
.ts-d8  { --ts-delay: 0.8s;  }
.ts-d9  { --ts-delay: 0.9s;  }
.ts-d10 { --ts-delay: 1s;    }

🟢 Any ts-* class activates transition automatically
🟢 ts-* (1–10) define duration
🟢 ts-* classes can be combined — last applied variable wins
🟢 Additional ts-* classes modify behavior (property, duration, ease, delay)
🟡 ts-2 assumes:
     - duration: 0.2s
     - easing: ease-in-out
     - property: all
     - delay: 0s
🟢 Supports both:
     - simple usage (ts-2)
     - advanced composition (ts-3 ts-ease ts-opacity ts-d4)
🔴 Avoid mixing too many transition targets (keep it focused)
🔴 Prefer ts-transform over ts-all for performance
🔴 Use transform system for movement instead of offset
🟢 Transition supports only visual properties
🟢 Focus on opacity, transform, color and effects
🔴 Layout properties are not supported (width, height, margin, padding)
🔴 Positional properties are not supported (top, left, right, bottom)
🔴 Avoid using transition for structural layout changes
🟡 System is intentionally restricted to ensure smooth performance and predictable UI behavior

@font-face {
  font-family: "Inter";
  src: url("fonts/Inter/Inter-VariableFont_opsz,wght.ttf")
       format("truetype");

  font-style: normal;
  font-display: swap;
}

       format("truetype");

  font-style: italic;
  font-display: swap;
}
