Font Family Utility Classes

.ff-main   { font-family: var(--ff-main); }
.ff-sub    { font-family: var(--ff-sub); }
.ff-acnt   { font-family: var(--ff-acnt); }
.ff-code   { font-family: var(--ff-code); }

.ff-system {
  font-family:
    system-ui,
    -apple-system,
    BlinkMacSystemFont,
    "Segoe UI",
    sans-serif;
}

🟢 ff-main demonstrates a locally hosted variable font and is typically used as the project's primary typeface
🟢 ff-sub demonstrates a Google Font and is useful for secondary typography and branding variations
🟡 Google Fonts already hosts the font files and generates the @font-face rules. So, No additional @font-face required.
🟢 ff-acnt demonstrates the traditional multi-file font approach using separate files for weights and styles
🟡 ff-acnt intentionally demonstrates a traditional font setup where only selected weights and styles are available
🟡 When a requested weight or style is unavailable, the browser automatically selects the closest available variation
🟢 ff-code uses a monospace font suitable for code snippets, technical content and identifiers
🟢 ff-system uses the operating system's native UI font for maximum compatibility and performance
🟡 Variable fonts can support multiple weights through a single file, while traditional fonts often require separate files for each weight or style
🔴 Missing local or online fonts will automatically fall back to the next font defined in the font stack

Font Size Variables

:root {
  --fz-xxs: 12px;
  --fz-xs : 14px;
  --fz-sm : 16px;
  --fz-md : 20px;
  --fz-lg : 24px;
  --fz-xl : 32px;
  --fz-xxl: 48px;  
  }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
  :root {
  --fz-xxs: 12px;
  --fz-xs : 14px;
  --fz-sm : 15px;
  --fz-md : 17px;
  --fz-lg : 20px;
  --fz-xl : 26px;
  --fz-xxl: 32px;
  }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
  :root {
  --fz-xxs: 11px;
  --fz-xs : 13px;
  --fz-sm : 14px;
  --fz-md : 16px;
  --fz-lg : 18px;
  --fz-xl : 22px;
  --fz-xxl: 26px;
  }
}

Font Sizes Utility Classes

.fz-xxs { font-size: var(--fz-xxs); }
.fz-xs  { font-size: var(--fz-xs);  }
.fz-sm  { font-size: var(--fz-sm);  }
.fz-md  { font-size: var(--fz-md);  }
.fz-lg  { font-size: var(--fz-lg);  }
.fz-xl  { font-size: var(--fz-xl);  }
.fz-xxl { font-size: var(--fz-xxl); }

🟢 fz-* utilities provide a semantic font-size scale (xxs → xxl).
🟢 Sizes adapt across breakpoints using responsive variable overrides.
🟢 fz-* numeric classes are available for precise fallback usage.
🟡 Prefer semantic sizing for consistency across components.
🔴 Avoid fixed px usage — breaks responsive behavior and system consistency.

Font Sizes Fallback rem Values

html {font-size: 16px;} 

@media (max-width: 990px) {
  html {font-size: 15px;}
}

@media (max-width: 770px) {
  html {font-size: 14px;}
}

🟢 Uses rem units so all sizes scale consistently across breakpoints.
🟡 Changing html font-size affects the entire typography fallback system — use with awareness.

.wsp--2  { word-spacing: -2px;  }
.wsp--1  { word-spacing: -1px;  }
.wsp-0   { word-spacing: 0;     }
.wsp-1   { word-spacing: 1px;   }
.wsp-2   { word-spacing: 2px;   }
.wsp-3   { word-spacing: 3px;   }
.wsp-4   { word-spacing: 4px;   }
.wsp-5   { word-spacing: 5px;   }
.wsp-6   { word-spacing: 6px;   }
.wsp-7   { word-spacing: 7px;   }
.wsp-8   { word-spacing: 8px;   }
.wsp-9   { word-spacing: 9px;   }
.wsp-10  { word-spacing: 10px;  }

🟢 lsp-* utilities control letter spacing using precise pixel values.
🟢 Useful for display typography, hero headings, badges, labels, and logos.
🟢 wsp-* utilities control spacing between words using precise pixel values.
🟢 Useful for typography refinement, headings, display text, and branding.
🟢 Supports both positive and negative tracking.
🟡 Large positive values may reduce readability in body text.
🟡 Large negative values may cause character overlap in certain fonts.
🟢 Prefer small adjustments (-2px to 2px) for most UI.

Modern Design System

User Select System

User Select Utility Classes

.uselect-auto { user-select: auto; }
.uselect-none { user-select: none; }
.uselect-all  { user-select: all;  }
.uselect-text { user-select: text; }

🟢 uselect-auto uses the browser's default text selection behavior
🟢 uselect-none prevents users from selecting text and is commonly used for buttons, controls and interactive UI elements
🟢 uselect-all automatically selects the entire content when any part is selected
🟢 uselect-text explicitly enables text selection and can override inherited restrictions
🟡 Use uselect-all for code snippets, URLs, API keys and copyable content
🟡 Use uselect-none sparingly as users often expect text to remain selectable
🔴 User selection utilities control selection behavior only and do not affect keyboard focus or accessibility

Live Demo

uselect-auto

Try selecting this text normally.

uselect-none

This text cannot be selected.

uselect-all npm install my-framework
uselect-text
Parent locked. "Select only this text".

Visibility System

Visibility Utility Classes

.vis-visible { visibility: visible; }
.vis-hidden  { visibility: hidden;  }
@media (max-width: 990px) {
.vis-tb-visible { visibility: visible; }
.vis-tb-hidden  { visibility: hidden;  }
}
@media (max-width: 770px) {
.vis-mb-visible { visibility: visible; }
.vis-mb-hidden  { visibility: hidden;  }
}

Hover Engine

[class*=" hs-vis-"]:hover,
[class^="hs-vis-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class^="hs-vis-"]:hover,
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" hs-vis-"]:hover {
  visibility: var(--hs-vis);
}

Hover Utility Classes

.hs-vis-hidden  { --hs-vis: hidden;  }
.hs-vis-visible { --hs-vis: visible; }

Child Hover Engine

.chs-parent:hover [class^="chs-vis-"],
.chs-parent:hover [class*=" chs-vis-"] {
  visibility: var(--chs-vis);
}

Child Hover Utility Classes

.chs-vis-hidden  { --chs-vis: hidden;  }
.chs-vis-visible { --chs-vis: visible; }

🟢 Base (vis-*) controls element visibility without affecting layout
🟢 Parent (chs-vis-*) applies on parent hover
🟢 Self (hs-vis-*) applies on hover and overrides parent
🟡 Last class wins when multiple utilities are used
🔴 visibility: hidden hides element but keeps layout space
🔴 visibility does not remove element from layout and may still affect interaction depending on context
🔴 Does not remove element from accessibility tree in all cases (use carefully)
🟢 visibility can be combined with opacity for smooth transitions

Width System

Variables

:root {
  --w-a-px: 0px;
  --w-a-p: 0%;
  --w-a-vw: 0vw;
  --w-a-min: 0px;
  --w-a-max: 0px;
}

Width Engine

[class^="w-"],[class*=" w-"] {
  width: var(--w-width, auto);
  max-width: var(--w-max, none);
  min-width: var(--w-min, auto);
}

Helper Classes

.w-auto { 
    width: auto;
    max-width: none;
    min-width: 0;
}

.w-fit { 
    width: fit-content;
    max-width: none;
    min-width: 0;
}

🟢 Any w-* class activates the width system
🟢 Each system (width / max / min) operates independently
🟢 Width is composed using a base (w-*) + additive values (w-add-*)
🟢 Use only one base class per element (do not mix w-*px, w-*p, w-*vw)
🟢 Add classes refine the nearest base value using 1-unit increments.
🟡 Only apply when needed — avoid unnecessary overrides
🟢 w-auto → default browser behavior, w-fit → content-based sizing
🟡 These override all width logic
🔴 Do not combine (w-auto, w-fit) with other w-* classes

px | min | max - Utility Classes

🟢 w-*px Uses true numeric composition and Supports precise values without extra classes
🟢 w-*min Ensures minimum size
🟢 w-*max Applies constraint only when used
🟢 w-*min and w-*max Works with all width types (% / vw / px)

% Utility Classes

.w-0p   { --w-width: 0%;                        }
.w-1p   { --w-width: 1%;                        }
.w-2p   { --w-width: 2%;                        }
.w-3p   { --w-width: 3%;                        }
.w-4p   { --w-width: 4%;                        }
.w-5p   { --w-width: 5%;                        }
.w-6p   { --w-width: 6%;                        }
.w-7p   { --w-width: 7%;                        }
.w-8p   { --w-width: 8%;                        }

Z-Index System

z-index Utility Classes

.z-0  { z-index: 0;  }
.z-1  { z-index: 1;  }
.z-2  { z-index: 2;  }
.z-3  { z-index: 3;  }
.z-4  { z-index: 4;  }
.z-5  { z-index: 5;  }
.z-6  { z-index: 6;  }
.z-7  { z-index: 7;  }
.z-8  { z-index: 8;  }
.z-9  { z-index: 9;  }
.z-10 { z-index: 10; }

🟢 z-* utilities control stacking order (which element appears on top).
🟢 Higher values appear above lower values.
🟢 Works only on positioned elements (requires pn-relative, pn-absolute, etc.).
🔴 z-index only affects stacking contexts and overlapping elements.
🟡 Most common usage is with positioned elements (pn-relative, pn-absolute, pn-fixed, pn-sticky).
🔴 Avoid large arbitrary values — keep stacking levels limited and predictable.

Live Demo

Z-index (Stacking Order)
Box A (z-1)
Box B (z-2)
Box C (z-3)

🟢 z-* controls which element appears on top.
🟢 Higher value = appears above others.
🟢 Works only on positioned elements.
🔴 Without positioning (pn-*), z-index has no effect.

Without positioning (z-index does NOT work)
Box A (z-1)
Box B (z-2)
Box C (z-3)

🔴 All elements use z-*, but none are positioned (no pn-*), so z-index has no effect.
🟡 Elements follow normal document flow instead of overlapping, so stacking cannot occur.
🟡 Offset utilities (top/left) also require positioning, so they are ignored here.
🟢 Apply pn-relative, pn-absolute, pn-fixed, or pn-sticky to activate stacking behavior.

Interactions

🟢 All interactions must be scoped using [data-ut].
🟢 Each [data-ut] acts as an independent component.
🔴 Never run JavaScript globally across the entire page.

Universal Pattern

// ✅ Step 1: Find each component
document.querySelectorAll('[data-ut]').forEach(scope => {

  // ✅ Step 2: Get items inside the component
  const items = scope.querySelectorAll(':scope > *');

  // ✅ Step 3: Apply interaction inside this component only
  items.forEach(item => {
    item.onclick = () => {

      // Example behavior (replace as needed)
      items.forEach(i => i.classList.remove('active'));
      item.classList.add('active');

    };
  });

});

🟡 This pattern does not define behavior.
🟡 It only ensures interactions stay inside the component.
🟢 You replace the logic depending on the use-case.

Behavior Examples

// Active (navigation, tabs)
// Only one item can be active
items.forEach(i => i.classList.remove('active'));
item.classList.add('active');
// Toggle (dropdown, accordion)
// Click to open/close
item.classList.toggle('open');
// Single selection (filters)
// Choose one option
items.forEach(i => i.classList.remove('selected'));
item.classList.add('selected');
// Multi selection (tags, filters)
// Toggle multiple options
item.classList.toggle('selected');

🟢 The selection pattern stays the same.
🟢 Only the state logic changes (active, open, selected).

States

🟢 Interaction system controls user behavior & feedback, not layout or spacing.
🟢 Uses Pseudo classes - :active, :hover, :focus for temporary interaction states.
🟢 Uses State classes - active, open, disabled, selected for persistent states.
🟡 State classes are controlled via JavaScript.
🟡 Works together with utility classes (spacing, colors, layout).

States - Pseudo Interaction

🟢 Pseudo states define temporary interaction behavior controlled by the browser.
🟢 They respond to user actions like hover, click, and keyboard navigation.
🟢 Always scope pseudo states using [data-ut] to avoid styling conflicts.
🟡 Pseudo states are short-lived and should not replace persistent state classes.
🔴 Do not rely on pseudo states for application logic or state management.

Available Pseudo States

🟢 :hover         – triggered when a pointer is over an element.
🟢 :active        – active during click/press interaction.
🟢 :focus         – triggered when element receives focus (mouse or keyboard).
🟢 :focus-visible – shows focus only when needed (keyboard navigation).
🟡 :checked       – works for form inputs (checkbox, radio).
🔴 :link/:visited – apply only to anchor links.
[data-ut]:hover         { ... }
[data-ut]:active        { ... }
[data-ut]:focus         { ... }
[data-ut]:focus-visible { ... }

Live Demo

Hover (move mouse) 
[data-ut="one"]:hover {
  background: var(--color-sub);
}

🟢 :hover → pointer interaction feedback. 

[data-ut="two"]:active {
  transform: scale(0.95);
}

🟢 :active → press/click feedback (very short duration). 

[data-ut="three"]:focus {
  outline: 3px solid var(--color-main);
}

🟢 :focus → triggered when element is selected (click or tab).
🟡 Stays visible until focus moves away.

Focus-visible Demo

🟢 :focus-visible → shown during keyboard navigation (Tab).
🟡 Use Tab key to navigate. Focus will remain inside this area using js.
🟡 Hidden on mouse click for cleaner UI.
🔴 Use Tab key to see this behavior. 

[data-demo="focus-area"] button:focus {
  outline: none;
}

[data-demo="focus-area"] button:focus-visible {
  outline: 3px solid var(--color-acnt);
  outline-offset: 3px;
  background: var(--color-sub);
  border: none;
}

States - class Interaction

🟢 State classes define persistent UI states controlled by the application.
🟢 Unlike pseudo states, these remain active until explicitly changed.
🟢 Typically managed through JavaScript or interactions (e.g., Webflow).
🟡 Always combine state classes with [data-ut] for proper scoping.
🔴 Do not rely on pseudo states (hover, focus) to represent UI state.

Available State Classes

🟢 .active   – indicates the currently active or focused item (nav, tab, etc.).
🟢 .open     – used for expandable elements (dropdowns, accordions).
🟢 .selected – marks chosen items (filters, options).
🟢 .disabled – prevents interaction and visually indicates inactivity.
[data-ut].active    { ... }
[data-ut].open      { ... }
[data-ut].selected  { ... }
[data-ut].disabled  { ... }

Active State – Scroll Demo

Base Color Spacing Typography

Base

Color

Spacing

Typography

🟢 .active updates based on scroll position.
🟢 Clicking navigation scrolls inside the container.
🔴 Uses relative positioning for accurate scroll detection. 

/* CSS */
        
  [data-ut="active-area"] a.active {
    background: var(--color-main);
    color: #fff;
    font-weight: 600;
  }
 /* HTML */

<div data-ut="active-area" class="flex-x h-400px of-hidden">

  <!-- ✅ NAV -->
  <div class="w-20p of-y-auto pa-xs">
    <a class="dis-block pa-xxs ra-xxs clr-g7 active" href="#demo-base">
      Base
    </a>
    <a class="dis-block pa-xxs ra-xxs clr-g7" href="#demo-color">
      Color
    </a>
    <a class="dis-block pa-xxs ra-xxs clr-g7" href="#demo-spacing">
      Spacing
    </a>
    <a class="dis-block pa-xxs ra-xxs clr-g7" href="#demo-typography">
      Typography
    </a>
  </div>

  <!-- ✅ SCROLL CONTAINER -->
  <aside class="w-80p of-y-auto pa-sm bg-g1">

    <section id="demo-base" class="h-300px ba-xxs mb-sm pa-sm">
      <h4>Base</h4>
    </section>

    <section id="demo-color" class="h-300px ba-xxs mb-sm pa-sm">
      <h4>Color</h4>
    </section>

    <section id="demo-spacing" class="h-300px ba-xxs mb-sm pa-sm">
      <h4>Spacing</h4>
    </section>

    <section id="demo-typography" class="h-300px ba-xxs mb-sm pa-sm">
      <h4>Typography</h4>
    </section>

  </aside>

</div>    
  
 /* JS */
    
document.querySelectorAll('[data-ut="active-area"]').forEach(scope => {

  const items = scope.querySelectorAll('a');
  const scrollBox = scope.querySelector('aside');

  const sections = Array.from(items).map(item =>
    scrollBox.querySelector(item.getAttribute('href'))
  );

  function onScroll() {

    const scrollPos = scrollBox.scrollTop + 120;
    let activeIndex = 0;

    sections.forEach((section, index) => {
      if (!section) return;

      const sectionTop = section.offsetTop - scrollBox.offsetTop;

      if (scrollPos >= sectionTop) {
        activeIndex = index;
      }
    });

    items.forEach(i => i.classList.remove('active'));
    if (items[activeIndex]) {
      items[activeIndex].classList.add('active');
    }
  }

  scrollBox.addEventListener('scroll', onScroll);
  onScroll();

  items.forEach(item => {
    item.addEventListener('click', e => {

      e.preventDefault();

      const target = scrollBox.querySelector(item.getAttribute('href'));
      if (!target) return;

      scrollBox.scrollTo({
        top: target.offsetTop - scrollBox.offsetTop,
        behavior: 'smooth'
      });

    });
  });

});

Open State – Accordion Demo

Base content...
Color content...
Spacing content...
Typography content...

🟢 .open controls visibility.
🟢 Uses semantic elements (article, button, section).
🟡 No generic div targeting.
🔴 Structure defines behavior. 

/* CSS */
    
[data-ut="open-area"] article>section {
  display: none;
}

[data-ut="open-area"] article.open>section {
  display: block;
}

[data-ut="open-area"] article.open>button {
  background: var(--color-main);
  color: #fff;
}
/* HTML */
    
<div data-ut="open-area" class="stack-y-xs">

  <!-- ✅ ITEM -->
  <article class="sw-sm ra-xs">
    <button class="pa-xxs w-100p ta-left cur-pointer bg-g1">Base</button>
    <section class="pa-xxs">Base content...</section>
  </article>

</div>
/* JS */
        
document.querySelectorAll('[data-ut="open-area"]').forEach(scope => {

  const items = scope.querySelectorAll(':scope > article');

  items.forEach(item => {

    const trigger = item.querySelector('button');

    trigger.onclick = () => {

      items.forEach(i => i.classList.remove('open'));
      item.classList.add('open');

    };

  });

});

Selected State – Filter Demo

🟢 .selected represents chosen options.
🟢 Click to toggle selection on/off.
🟡 Multiple selections allowed.
🔴 Uses semantic inputs (checkbox) for state. 

/* CSS */

[data-ut="selected-area"] label.selected {
  background: var(--color-main);
  color: #fff;
  border-color: var(--color-main);
}
/* HTML */

<div data-ut="selected-area">

  <label>
    <input type="checkbox" hidden>
    Base
  </label>

</div>
/* JS */

document.querySelectorAll('[data-ut="selected-area"]').forEach(scope => {

  const items = scope.querySelectorAll('label');

  items.forEach(item => {

    const input = item.querySelector('input');

    item.onclick = () => {

      input.checked = !input.checked;

      item.classList.toggle('selected', input.checked);

    };

  });

});

Disabled State – Form Demo

🟢 .disabled prevents interaction.
🟢 Button enables when input has value.
🟡 State reflects input condition.
🔴 Interaction is blocked using CSS (pointer-events). 

/* CSS */

[data-ut="disabled-area"] button.disabled {
  pointer-events: none;
  cursor: not-allowed;
}
/* HTML */

<form data-ut="disabled-area">

  <input type="text" placeholder="Enter name">

  <button class="disabled">
    Submit
  </button>

</form>
/* JS */

document.querySelectorAll('[data-ut="disabled-area"]').forEach(scope => {

  const input = scope.querySelector('input');
  const button = scope.querySelector('button');

  input.oninput = () => {

    if (input.value.trim() !== '') {
      button.classList.remove('disabled');
    } else {
      button.classList.add('disabled');
    }

  };

});
